\left(\bm{V}^{T}\bm{W}\bm{V}\right)\bm{u}=\bm{V}^{T}\bm{W}(\bm{z}-\bm{\mu}_{z})
must hold as well, and acts
as a constraint. Moreover,
\bm{g}_{1}
and
\bm{g}_{2}

depend on
\bm{\sigma}
through
\bm{W}
and
\bm{u}
.
Also note that
H(j,\bm{c})
in (
A.6
)
and (
A.7
) is the distribution of

Z\sim H_{0}
but with its
j
-th

component fixed at the constant
c_{j}
. It is
thus a degenerate distribution concentrated on
the hyperplane
z_{j}=c_{j}
.

To derive the IF of the cellMR estimates, other important
pieces are the IFs of the functionals

\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H)
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H)
corresponding to the
MCD estimator of location and covariance
\bm{\mu}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}

applied to
\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1},\dots,\bm{\widehat{u}}_{n}
under both FDCM and FICM.
That is,
\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H)
corresponds
to the MCD functional
\bm{\Sigma}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(\cdot)

of scatter with parameter
0.5<\alpha<1
, applied
to the distribution
H^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T}(H))
of

\bm{u}
when
\bm{z}
is distributed as
H
, for

\bm{T}(H):=\left(\vect(\bm{V}(H)),\bm{\mu}_{z}(H),\bm{\sigma}(H)\right)^{T}
. Similarly,

\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H)
is the MCD functional

\bm{\mu}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(\cdot)
of location with parameter

\alpha
applied to
H^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T}(H))
.

Proposition 7
.

The casewise and cellwise influence functions
of
\vect(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}})
and
\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}
are

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}),H_{0})=

\displaystyle-\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\Big[\bm{S}^{\bm{u}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\;\;+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)+\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}(\Delta_{\bm{c}},\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},q_{\alpha,0})\Big]

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},H_{0})=

\displaystyle-\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\Big[\bm{S}^{\bm{u}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)+\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}(\Delta^{\bm{u}}_{\bm{c}},\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},q_{\alpha,0})\Big],

and

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}),H_{0})=

\displaystyle-\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\Big[\bm{S}^{\bm{u}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\;\;+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\;\;+d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}(H(j,\bm{c}),\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},q_{\alpha,0})\Big]\,,

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},H_{0})=

\displaystyle-\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\Big[\bm{S}^{\bm{u}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)+d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}(H(j,\bm{c}),\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0},q_{\alpha,0})\Big].

with
\bm{\sigma}(H)=\left(\sigma_{1,1}(H),\dots,\sigma_{1,d}(H),\sigma_{2}(H)\right)^{T}
,

\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}:=\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{0})
,

\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}:=\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{0})
,

\bm{T}_{0}:=\left(\vect(\bm{V}_{0}),\bm{\mu}_{z,0},\bm{\sigma}_{0}\right)^{T}
and

\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{1}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})\\
\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{2}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})\\
g^{\bm{u}}_{3}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})\end{array}\right].

(A.10)

The functions
\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{1}
,
\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{2}
, and

g^{\bm{u}}_{3}
are defined as

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{1}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\vect\left(\E_{H^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T})}\right.\Big[I\left({\bm{u}\in A(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})}\right)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\left.\left(c_{\alpha}\left(\bm{u}-\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)^{T}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)\Big]\right)\,,

\bm{g}^{\bm{u}}_{2}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})=\E_{H^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T})}\Big[I\left({\bm{u}\in A(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)\Big],

and

g^{\bm{u}}_{3}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})=\E_{H^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T})}\Big[I\left({\bm{u}\in A(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})}\right)-(1-\alpha)\Big]

where

\displaystyle A(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},q_{\alpha})=\{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\left(\bm{u}-\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)^{T}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)^{-1}\left(\bm{u}-\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\right)\leqslant q_{\alpha}\}.

The functional
q_{\alpha}(H)
satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}I\left({\bm{u}\in A(\bm{\Sigma}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H),\bm{\mu}^{\bm{u}}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H),q_{\alpha}(H))}\right)dH^{\bm{u}}(H,\bm{T})(\bm{u})=1-\alpha,

in which
q_{\alpha,0}:=q_{\alpha}(H_{0})
and

c_{\alpha}
is chosen in such a way that
consistency is obtained at a prespecified model.
The matrices
\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{1}
,

\bm{B}^{\bm{u}}_{2}
,
\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{1}
and
\bm{D}^{\bm{u}}_{2}
are
defined in
Centofanti et al. 2025
.

Proposition 8
.

The casewise and cellwise influence functions
of
\vect(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}})
are

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}),H_{0}\right)=

\displaystyle\;\bm{D}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}\Big[\bm{B}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+\bm{S}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+\bm{g}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(\Delta_{\bm{c}},\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{z}^{\perp},0})\Big]

and

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}),H_{0}\right)=

\displaystyle\;\bm{D}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}\Big[\bm{B}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}_{1}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)+\bm{B}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}_{2}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+\bm{S}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\bm{c},\bm{\sigma},H_{0}\right)+d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\bm{g}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H\left(j,\bm{c}\right),\bm{T}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{z}^{\perp},0})\Big],

with
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{z}^{\perp},0}=\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H_{0})
,

\bm{T}_{0}=\left(\vect(\bm{V}_{0}),\bm{\mu}_{z,0},\bm{\sigma}_{0}\right)^{T}
and

\bm{g}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H,\bm{T},\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}):=\vect\Big(\E_{H}\Big[b\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}-w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{z}-\bm{\mu}_{z}-\bm{V}\bm{u})(\bm{z}-\bm{\mu}_{z}-\bm{V}\bm{u})^{T}\bm{\widetilde{W}}\Big]\Big),

where
\bm{T}=\left(\vect(\bm{V}),\bm{\mu}_{z},\bm{\sigma}\right)^{T}
,

b=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}w_{j}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}w_{\ell}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}/d^{2}
,

\bm{u}
depends on
\bm{z}
and
\bm{T}
through (
B
).
The matrices
\bm{D}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}
,
\bm{B}_{1}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}
,

\bm{B}_{2}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}
, and
\bm{S}^{\bm{z}^{\perp}}
are
defined in
Centofanti et al. 2025
.

Proof of Proposition
1
.

Let us introduce the functionals
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\Sigma}
corresponding to
\bm{\widehat{\mu}}
and
\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}
defined in (
11
), which can be written by using (
7
) as

\bm{\mu}(H)=\bm{\mu}_{z}(H)+\bm{V}(H)\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H),\quad\bm{\Sigma}(H)=\bm{V}(H)\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H)(\bm{V}(H))^{T}+\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H).

(A.11)

Define the selection matrices
\bm{S}_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d}
and

\bm{S}_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
such that

\bm{\mu}_{x}(H)=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}(H)
,
\bm{\mu}_{y}(H)=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}(H)
and

\bm{\Sigma}_{x}(H)=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{x}^{T}
,
\bm{\Sigma}_{xy}(H)=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{y}^{T}
, where
\bm{\mu}_{x}
,
\bm{\mu}_{y}
,
\bm{\Sigma}_{x}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{xy}
represent the usual partitions of
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\Sigma}
.
From the definition of
\bm{\widehat{b}}
and
\bm{\widehat{B}}
in
(
2
) we have

\bm{B}(H)=\bigl(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{x}^{T}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{y}^{T}),\qquad\bm{b}(H)=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}(H)-(\bm{B}(H))^{T}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}(H)).

(A.12)

Let
\bm{A}(H):=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{x}^{T}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}
and

\bm{C}(H):=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}(H)\bm{S}_{y}^{T}
, so that
\bm{B}(H)=\bm{A}(H)^{-1}\bm{C}(H)
.
Using the identity

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{A}(H_{\varepsilon})^{-1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{A}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1},

we obtain

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{A}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\bm{C}_{0}+\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{C}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where
\bm{A}_{0}:=\bm{A}(H_{0})
and
\bm{C}_{0}:=\bm{C}(H_{0})
.
Equivalently, using
\bm{B}_{0}:=\bm{B}(H_{0})=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\bm{C}_{0}
,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{A}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{B}_{0}+\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{C}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Since

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{A}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{S}_{x}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{S}_{x}^{T},\qquad\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{C}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{S}_{x}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{S}_{y}^{T},

it follows that

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}\bm{S}_{x}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bigl(\bm{S}_{y}^{T}-\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\bm{B}_{0}\bigr).

Finally, vectorizing and using
\vect(\bm{M}\bm{X}\bm{N})=(\bm{N}^{T}\otimes\bm{M})\vect(\bm{X})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\bigl(\bm{S}_{y}-\bm{B}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\bigr)\otimes((\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{S}_{x})\Bigr)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{B}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

with
\bm{\Sigma}_{0}:=\bm{\Sigma}(H_{0})
, and
\bm{R}_{B}:=\bigl(\bm{S}_{y}-\bm{B}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\bigr)\otimes((\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{S}_{x})
.

Now consider
\bm{b}(H)
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{b}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{S}_{y}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bigl(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

For the second term, applying the product rule yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\bm{\mu}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{q}\bigr)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})^{T}\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{B}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

with
\bm{\mu}_{0}:=\bm{\mu}(H_{0})
.
Define the permutation matrix satisfying
\vect(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})^{T})=\bm{P}_{B}\vect(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon}))
. Then

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{b}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\bm{S}_{y}-\bm{B}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}\Bigr)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigl(\bm{\mu}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{q}\bigr)\bm{P}_{B}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{b,1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-\bm{R}_{b,2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where
\bm{R}_{b,1}:=\bm{S}_{y}-\bm{B}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}
and
\bm{R}_{b,2}:=\bigl(\bm{\mu}_{0}^{T}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{q}\bigr)\bm{P}_{B}
.

By using (
A.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)^{T}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{z}^{\perp}}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\bm{V}_{0}^{T}+\bm{V}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{V}_{0}^{T}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)^{T}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\;,

where
\bm{V}_{0}:=\bm{V}(H_{0})
, and

\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}:=\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{0})
.
Applying
\vect
to both sides yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{\Sigma}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\otimes\bm{I}_{d}\right)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\left(\bm{V}_{0}\otimes\bm{V}_{0}\right)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\left(\bm{I}_{d}\otimes\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\right)\bm{P}_{V}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\left(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where the matrix
\bm{P}_{V}
is a permutation
matrix such that

\vect\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)^{T}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)=\bm{P}_{V}\vect\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\left(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)
.
The matrices
\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}
and
\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}
are

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\otimes\bm{I}_{d}\right)+\left(\bm{I}_{d}\otimes\bm{V}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\right)\bm{P}_{V},

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{0}\otimes\bm{V}_{0}.

Similarly, by (
A.11
), we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}_{z}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\Bigl(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\Bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}_{z}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}+\bm{V}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}_{z}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left(\left(\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\right)^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{d}\right)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\bm{V}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{\mu}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{V}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\bm{V}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}_{z}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where
\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}=\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H_{0})

and
\bm{R}_{\mu}=\left(\left(\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD},0}\right)^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{d}\right)
.

So under the FDCM, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{B}\,\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{\Sigma}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{B}\Bigg[\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{V}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{B}\Big[\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}),H_{0}\right)+\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}),H_{0}\right)\Big].

Similarly

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{b}\bigl(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{b,1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}\bigl(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-\bm{R}_{b,2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{b,1}\Bigg[\bm{R}_{\mu}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{V}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\bm{V}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}\bigl(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\mu}_{z}\bigl(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\Bigg]-\bm{R}_{b,2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{b,1}\Big[\bm{R}_{\mu}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)+\bm{V}_{0}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)\Big]-\bm{R}_{b,2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{B}),H_{0}\right).

Using similar arguments, we obtain for the FICM

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\vect\bigl(\bm{B}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}))\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{B}\Big[\bm{R}_{\Sigma,1}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\bm{R}_{\Sigma,2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\Sigma}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}}),H_{0}\right)+\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{\bm{z}^{\perp}}),H_{0}\right)\Big],

and

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{b}\bigl(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c})\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{b,1}\Big[\bm{R}_{\mu}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{V}),H_{0}\right)+\bm{V}_{0}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}^{u}_{\text{\tiny MCD}},H_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.0pt+\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\bm{\mu}_{z},H_{0}\right)\Big]-\bm{R}_{b,2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\!\left(\bm{c},\vect(\bm{B}),H_{0}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
More on the construction of FastCellCov

Section
4.2
introduced the
FastCellCov auxiliary estimator. It
starts by applying cellCov to the
actual sample
\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{1},\dots,\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{n}
.
Now consider another sample

\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{1}^{*},\dots,\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{n}^{*}
,
that may be a bootstrap sample or a simulated
sample of size
n
generated from
F_{\bm{\theta}}
.
We then standardize it by computing

\bm{z}_{i}^{*}=\bm{\widehat{D}}^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{z}}^{*}_{i}
where

\bm{\widehat{D}}
is the diagonal matrix of scale
estimators of the original sample, as in
Section
2.1
.

To compute fitted values
\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\,*}
from
the
\bm{z}_{i}^{*}
, we first need to
construct an outlier-free version

\bm{z}_{i,\mathrm{imp}}^{*}
of
\bm{z}_{i}^{*}
.
We first standardize each
\bm{z}_{i}^{*}
to

\bm{z}_{s,i}^{*}=\bm{\widehat{S}}^{-1}\bigl(\bm{z}_{i}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z}),

(A.13)

where
\bm{\widehat{S}}:=\diag(s_{1},\ldots,s_{d})
with

s_{j}:=\sqrt{(\bm{\widehat{D}}^{-1}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{\widehat{D}}^{-1})_{jj}}
.
Then we compute the filtering weights vector

\bm{w}^{f}_{i}=(w^{f}_{i1},\dots,w^{f}_{id})^{T}
, where

w^{f}_{ij}=w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(z_{s,ij}^{*})
.
Next we compute the predicted vectors

\bm{\widehat{z}}_{s,i}^{\,*}=(\widehat{z}_{s,i1}^{\,*},\dots,\widehat{z}_{s,id}^{\,*})^{T}
as

\widehat{z}_{s,ij}^{\,*}\;=\;\frac{1}{\sum_{h\in H_{j}}|\mathrm{corr}_{jh}|\,w^{f}_{ih}}\;\,\sum_{h\in H_{j}}|\mathrm{corr}_{jh}|\,w^{f}_{ih}\,b_{jh}\,z_{s,ih}^{*},

(A.14)

which is a weighted mean of the terms

b_{jh}z_{s,ih}^{*}
. We obtain the
b_{jh}

from the Detecting Deviating Cells (DDC) method,
used in the initialization of cellCov. The

b_{jh}
is the DDC-estimated slope from a robust
no-intercept regression of variable
j
on
variable
h
. We also use the DDC-estimated
absolute correlation
\mathrm{corr}_{jh}
between
variables
j
and
h
, combined with

\omega^{f}_{ih}
. The index set

H_{j}
contains all variables
h
for which

|\mathrm{corr}_{jh}|\geqslant 0.5
.

Note that the prediction (
A.14
)
typically shrinks the scale of the entries.
To correct for this, we compute the slope

a^{shr}_{j}
of a robust no-intercept regression
of the observed
z_{s,ij}^{*}
on the predicted
values
\widehat{z}_{s,ij}^{\,*}
, and then rescale
the predictions by setting all
\widehat{z}_{s,ij}^{\,*}\leftarrow a^{shr}_{j}\widehat{z}_{s,ij}^{\,*}
.

We then compute the standardized cell residuals

r_{ij}^{*}=\frac{z_{s,ij}^{*}-\widehat{z}_{s,ij}^{\,*}}{s_{r,j}}\;,

(A.15)

where
s_{r,j}=\sigma_{M}(\{z_{ij}-\widehat{z}_{ij}\}_{i=1}^{n})
was computed
on the observed sample.
Next we compute the residual weights vector

\bm{w}^{r}_{i}=(w^{r}_{i1},\dots,w^{r}_{id})^{T}
, where

w^{r}_{ij}=w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(r_{ij}^{*})
. Finally we set

\bm{z}_{i,\mathrm{imp}}^{*}=(\bm{w}^{f}_{i}\odot\bm{w}^{r}_{i}\odot\bm{m}_{i}^{*})\odot\bm{z}_{i}^{*}+(\bm{1}_{d}-\bm{w}^{f}_{i}\odot\bm{w}^{r}_{i}\odot\bm{m}_{i}^{*})\odot\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{s,i}^{*}\;,

(A.16)

with
\bm{m}_{i}^{*}
the missing indicator vector
whose entries are 0 for missing and 1 otherwise,
and
\bm{\widetilde{z}}_{s,i}^{*}=\bm{\widehat{S}}\bm{\widehat{z}}_{s,i}^{\,*}+\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z}
.
Then we compute
\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\,*}:=\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z}+\bm{\widehat{V}}\bm{\widehat{V}}^{T}(\bm{z}_{i,\mathrm{imp}}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z})
.

Next we compute the matrix

\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{*}=\bm{W}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}_{*}\odot\bm{M}_{*}
, where the

n\times d
matrix
\bm{W}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}_{*}
contains the
weights
w_{*,ij}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}=w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}\left(\frac{\widehat{r}_{ij}^{\,*}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{1,j}^{*}}\right)

with
\widehat{r}_{ij}^{\,*}=z_{ij}^{*}-\widehat{z}_{ij}^{\,*}
and

\widehat{\sigma}_{1,j}=\sigma_{M}(\{\widehat{r}_{ij}\}_{i=1}^{n})

was already computed on the observed sample.
The
n\times d
matrix
\bm{M}_{*}
contains the
missingness indicators of

\bm{z}_{1}^{*},\dots,\bm{z}_{n}^{*}
. Next we compute
the new center

\bm{\widetilde{\mu}}_{F}:=(\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}})^{-1}\odot\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{i}^{*}\bm{z}_{i}^{*}\,,

(A.17)

where
\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{i}^{*}
is a diagonal matrix whose diagonal
is the
i
-th row of
\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{*}
and

\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}=\diag(c^{\bm{\mu}}_{1},\dots,c^{\bm{\mu}}_{d})

with
c^{\bm{\mu}}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}w_{*,ij}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}m_{ij}^{*}
.
We also compute the new covariance

\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{F}:=(\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}})^{-1}\odot\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{i}^{*}(\bm{z}_{i}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z})(\bm{z}_{i}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z})^{T}\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{i}^{*},

(A.18)

where
\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}}=\{c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}\}
with

c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{ij}^{*}m_{i\ell}^{*}w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}w_{*,ij}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}w_{*,i\ell}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}
.
The weights
w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}

in (
A.17
) and (
A.18
)
are given by

w_{*,i}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}=w^{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}\left(\frac{\widehat{r}_{*,i}^{\,\mathrm{imp}}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\right)

where
\widehat{r}_{*,i}^{\;\mathrm{imp}}
is given by

\widehat{r}_{*,i}^{\;\mathrm{imp}}:=\frac{1}{\sum_{j=1}^{d}m_{*,ij}w_{*,ij}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}}\;\sum_{j=1}^{d}m_{*,ij}w_{*,ij}^{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\widehat{r}_{ij}^{\,*})^{2}

(A.19)

and
\widehat{\sigma}_{2}=\sigma_{M}(\widehat{r}_{i}^{\;\mathrm{imp}})

was computed on the observed sample.

As
w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{case}}
and

w_{*,ij}^{\mathrm{cell}}
measure the outlyingness
of
\bm{z}_{i}^{*}
in the principal subspace formed
by
\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z}
and
\bm{\widehat{V}}
, we included
weights
w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}

in (
A.17
) and (
A.18
) to
limit the influence of potentially outlying fitted
values
\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\;*}
. Let
d_{i,\mathrm{mah}}^{2}:=(\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\,*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}})^{T}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}}^{-1}(\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\,*}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}})
denote the robust squared
Mahalanobis distance of
\bm{\widehat{z}}_{i}^{\,*}
from the robust
location and scatter estimates
\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}}
and

\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}}
, obtained by applying the MCD
estimator to the fitted values computed on the
observed sample, as in (
7
).
The subspace weights are defined as

w_{*,i}^{\mathrm{sub}}=\frac{\psi^{s}\!\left(d^{2}_{i,\mathrm{mah}}\right)}{d^{2}_{i,\mathrm{mah}}},

where
\psi^{s}
is the derivative of the hyperbolic
tangent
\rho
-function defined in Supplementary
Material
A
.

The final FastCellCov estimates
\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{F}
and

\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{F}
of
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\Sigma}
are given by

\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{F}:=\bm{\widehat{D}}\bm{\widetilde{\mu}}_{F}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{F}:=\bm{\widehat{D}}\bm{\widetilde{\Sigma}}_{F}\bm{\widehat{D}}.

(A.20)

Appendix D
Proofs of asymptotic exactness of cellBoot

Throughout the paper, for sequences of random variables
(X_{n})
and positive
deterministic sequences
(a_{n})
, we use the following asymptotic notation:

•

X_{n}=o_{p}(a_{n})
if
X_{n}/a_{n}\rightarrow_{p}0
;

•

X_{n}=O_{p}(a_{n})
if for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
M<\infty
such that

\Pr(|X_{n}|/a_{n}>M)<\varepsilon
for all sufficiently large
n
.

•

X_{n}=o(a_{n})
if
X_{n}/a_{n}\rightarrow 0
.

We write
X_{n}\rightarrow_{p}X
for convergence in probability.
For any event
A
, we denote by
I_{A}
its indicator function, that is,

I_{A}:=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }A\text{ occurs},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In particular, for any integrable random vector
X
and any event
A
,

\E[X]=\E[XI_{A}]+\E[XI_{A^{c}}]=\Pr(A)\,\E[X\mid A]+\Pr(A^{c})\,\E[X\mid A^{c}],

where
A^{c}
denotes the complement of the event
A
.
The bootstrap probability measure
{\Pr}^{*}
denotes probability computed
under the bootstrap distribution, conditional on the observed data

\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
. Correspondingly,
\E^{*}(\cdot)
and
\Cov^{*}(\cdot)
denote
expectation and covariance with respect to
{\Pr}^{*}
.

The admissible parameter space is defined as

\bm{\Theta}=\Bigl\{\,(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}:\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{S}^{d},\ \|\bm{\mu}\|\leqslant M,\ c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant C\Bigr\},

where
M<\infty
and
0<c\leqslant C<\infty
.
Here,
\mathbb{S}^{d}
denotes the space of symmetric
d\times d
matrices.
The operator
\vecth_{s}(\cdot)
denotes the
scaled half–vectorization

defined as follows: for
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
,

\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})
is obtained by stacking the lower triangular elements
(including the diagonal), and multiplying each off–diagonal element by
\sqrt{2}
.
With this convention, the Euclidean norm of
\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})
coincides
with the Frobenius norm of
\bm{\Sigma}
, consequently, for any
(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}^{d}
,

\|(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\|^{2}=\|\bm{\mu}\|^{2}+\|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})\|^{2},

so that the standard Euclidean geometry on

\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
corresponds exactly to the natural
Frobenius geometry on the covariance matrix component.
Hence,
\bm{\Theta}
is a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
whose last
d(d+1)/2
components,
when reshaped and rescaled into a symmetric
d\times d
matrix, form a matrix
with eigenvalues bounded between
c
and
C
.
The interior of
A
is defined as

\interior(A):=\Bigl\{x\in A:\exists\,\delta>0\text{ such that}\ x+h\in A\ \text{for all }h\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\ \text{with }\|h\|<\delta\Bigr\}.

Let us consider
\bm{t}=(\bm{a},\vecth(\bm{L}))^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
,
where
\bm{L}=\bm{Q}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{Q}^{T}
, with
\bm{Q}
the
d\times d
orthogonal matrix whose columns are the eigenvectors of
\bm{L}
, and
\bm{\Lambda}=\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})
the diagonal matrix containing the corresponding eigenvalues.
The operator
\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}
projects
\bm{t}
onto
\bm{\Theta}
and is defined as

\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(\bm{t})=(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{T})^{T},

where

\tilde{\bm{\mu}}=\begin{cases}\bm{a},&\text{if }\|\bm{a}\|\leqslant M,\\[3.0pt]
\dfrac{M}{\|\bm{a}\|}\,\bm{a},&\text{if }\|\bm{a}\|>M,\end{cases}\qquad\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}=\bm{Q}\bm{\Lambda}_{c}\bm{Q}^{T},

and
\bm{\Lambda}_{c}=\mathrm{diag}(\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\lambda}_{d})
with

\tilde{\lambda}_{j}=\min\{\max\{\lambda_{j},c\},C\},\qquad j=1,\ldots,d.

A bootstrap sample is obtained by resampling from the empirical
distribution
\hat{F}
, and the corresponding bootstrap version of

\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
is denoted by
\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
. Then, the bootstrap estimator
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}
is

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}=\argzero_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl\{\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\bigr\}.

Let
\bm{x}_{1,\bm{\theta}},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n,\bm{\theta}}
be a random sample generated from the distribution

F_{\bm{\theta}}
, with parameter
\bm{\theta}=(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}
.
We rewrite the FastCellCov estimator of the location as

\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{F,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\Big(\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\Big)^{-1}\odot\sum_{i=1}^{n}w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},

where
\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})=\diag(\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{i1,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\dots,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{id,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}))
, and
\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
is the diagonal matrix with entries, for
j=1,\dots,d
,

\big(\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)_{jj}:=\max\!\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{ij,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\,n\tilde{\delta}\right\}.

Note that the diagonal elements of the summed denominator matrix are clipped
at
n\tilde{\delta}
, with
\tilde{\delta}>0
, making explicit the regularization step that is implicitly applied in the FastCellCov estimator. This prevents division by arbitrarily small values and ensures numerical stability.
We make explicit the dependence on

\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}
, namely the tuning parameters that determine
the weights

w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})
,

w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})
,
and the diagonal matrices

\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})
associated with each observation

\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\bm{\eta}=\left(\diag(\bm{\widehat{D}})^{T},\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z}^{T},\right.

\displaystyle\vecth(\bm{\widehat{V}}\bm{\widehat{V}}^{T})^{T},\diag(\bm{\widehat{S}})^{T},\vecth_{-d}(\bm{B}^{slope})^{T},

\displaystyle\vecth_{-d}(\bm{C}^{corr})^{T},(\bm{a}^{shr})^{T},\bm{s}_{r}^{T},(\bm{\widehat{\sigma}}^{*})^{T},\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}}^{T},\vecth_{-d}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{\bm{\widehat{z}}})^{T}\left.\right)^{T},

where
\bm{B}^{slope}=\{b_{jh}\}
,
\bm{C}^{corr}=\{corr_{jh}\}
,

\bm{a}^{shr}=(a^{shr}_{1},\dots,a^{shr}_{d})^{T}
,

\bm{s}_{r}=(s_{r,1},\dots,s_{r,d})^{T}
, and

\bm{\widehat{\sigma}}^{*}=(\widehat{\sigma}^{*}_{1,1},\dots,\widehat{\sigma}^{*}_{1,d},\widehat{\sigma}^{*}_{2})^{T}
.
Here,
\vecth(\cdot)
denotes the half–vectorization operator, mapping a
symmetric matrix to the vector obtained by stacking its lower triangular
elements (including the diagonal), while
\vecth_{-d}(\cdot)
denotes the
half–vectorization without the diagonal, obtained by stacking only the
strictly lower triangular elements of a symmetric matrix.
Analogously, the FastCellCov estimator of the scatter matrix is

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{F,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\Big(

\displaystyle\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\Big)^{-1}\odot

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z})\,(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{z})^{T}\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),

where
\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\{c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\}
is defined elementwise by

c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\max\!\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{ij,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{i\ell,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\,n\tilde{\delta}\right\}.

Let

\displaystyle a_{\mu}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle:=w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},

\displaystyle b_{\mu}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle:=\diag\!\big(\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\big),

\displaystyle a_{\Sigma}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle:=\vecth_{s}\Big(w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\mu})\,(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\mu})^{T}\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\Big),

\displaystyle b_{\Sigma}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle:=\vecth_{s}\big(\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\big),

where

\bm{C}^{\bm{\mu}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})=w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\bm{\widetilde{W}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}).

and
\bm{C}^{\bm{\Sigma}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})=\{c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\}
is defined elementwise by

c^{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j\ell}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})=w^{\mathrm{case}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,w^{\mathrm{sub}}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{ij,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})\,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{cell}}(x_{i\ell,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}).

We now collect the location and scatter components into the vector-valued
functions

a_{F}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}):=\bigl(a_{\mu}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})^{T},\;a_{\Sigma}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})^{T}\bigr)^{T},\qquad b_{F}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}):=\bigl(b_{\mu}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})^{T},\;b_{\Sigma}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})^{T}\bigr)^{T}.

Write

\bar{a}_{F,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{F}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\bar{b}_{F,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{F}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}).

With this notation, the
FastCellCov estimator
\widehat{P}_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
of
(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}
is defined componentwise,
for
j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
, as

\big(\widehat{P}_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\big)_{j}:=\begin{cases}\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{F,n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{(\bar{b}_{F,n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})},&\big|(\bar{b}_{F,n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant\tilde{\delta},\\[6.0pt]
\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{F,n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\tilde{\delta}},&\big|(\bar{b}_{F,n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|<\tilde{\delta}.\end{cases}

Finally, the corresponding population target is

P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),

where
\oslash
denotes the elementwise (Hadamard) division of two vectors of the same
dimension,

\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\E[a_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})],\qquad\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\E[b_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})],

and
\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}}
denotes a generic random vector from
F_{\bm{\theta}}
.

Lemma 1
.

The set
\bm{\Theta}
is closed and convex.

Proof.

To prove convexity, let

(\bm{\mu}_{1}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})^{T})^{T}
and

(\bm{\mu}_{2}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2})^{T})^{T}

be two elements of
\bm{\Theta}
, and let
t\in[0,1]
.
Define

\bm{\mu}_{t}=t\bm{\mu}_{1}+(1-t)\bm{\mu}_{2},\qquad\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})=t\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})+(1-t)\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}).

By convexity of the Euclidean norm,

\|\bm{\mu}_{t}\|\leqslant t\|\bm{\mu}_{1}\|+(1-t)\|\bm{\mu}_{2}\|\leqslant M,

so
\bm{\mu}_{t}
satisfies
\|\bm{\mu}_{t}\|\leqslant M
.

We have
\bm{\Sigma}_{t}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
.
We now verify that the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}_{t}
remain within
[c,C]
.
It is well known that the maps

\bm{\Sigma}\mapsto\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})

and
\bm{\Sigma}\mapsto\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})

are, respectively, concave and convex on the space of symmetric matrices.
Hence, for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})\geqslant t\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})+(1-t)\,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}),\qquad\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})\leqslant t\,\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})+(1-t)\,\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}).

It follows that

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})\geqslant c,\qquad\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})\leqslant C.

Therefore,

(\bm{\mu}_{t}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{t})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}
,
proving that
\bm{\Theta}
is convex.

To prove closedness, let

\{(\bm{\mu}_{n}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})^{T})^{T}\}

be a convergent sequence in
\bm{\Theta}
, with

(\bm{\mu}_{n}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})^{T})^{T}\rightarrow(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}.

We show that
(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}
.
Since the Euclidean norm is continuous and
\|\bm{\mu}_{n}\|\leqslant M
for all
n
,
passing to the limit gives
\|\bm{\mu}\|\leqslant M
.
Moreover,
\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\rightarrow\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})
implies that

\bm{\Sigma}_{n}\rightarrow\bm{\Sigma}
in the matrix entries.
Since each
\bm{\Sigma}_{n}
is symmetric, the limit
\bm{\Sigma}
is also symmetric.
The eigenvalues of symmetric matrices depend continuously on the matrix entries.
Hence,

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\rightarrow\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}),\qquad\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\rightarrow\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}).

Because
c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma}_{n})\leqslant C

for all
n
, continuity implies

c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant C.

Thus
(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}
, showing that
\bm{\Theta}
is closed.
∎

Lemma 2
.

For any
\bm{t}=(\bm{a}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{L})^{T})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
,
define

\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(\bm{t})=(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{T})^{T}.

Then
\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}
is the metric projection of
\bm{t}
onto
\bm{\Theta}

with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|
.

Proof.

We seek the metric projection of

\bm{t}=(\bm{a}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{L})^{T})^{T}
onto
\bm{\Theta}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|
, i.e.,

\min_{\bm{p}=(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bm{p}-(\bm{a}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{L})^{T})^{T}\right\|^{2}.

By definition of
\|\cdot\|
, this equals

\min_{(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl(\|\bm{\mu}-\bm{a}\|^{2}+\|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})-\vect(\bm{L})\|^{2}\bigr).

Since
\bm{\Theta}
is the Cartesian product

\{\bm{\mu}:\|\bm{\mu}\|\leqslant M\}\times\{\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}):\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{S}^{d},c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant C\},

and the objective is separable, the minimization splits into two independent parts.
The problem

\min_{\bm{\mu}:\|\bm{\mu}\|\leqslant M}\ \|\bm{\mu}-\bm{a}\|^{2}

is the Euclidean projection of
\bm{a}
onto the ball of radius
M
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
which yields the stated
\tilde{\bm{\mu}}
.
The problem

\min_{\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}):c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant C}\ \|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})-\vect(\bm{L})\|^{2}

is the projection of
\bm{L}
onto the set of symmetric matrices whose eigenvalues lie in
[c,C]
.
Let
\bm{L}=\bm{Q}\,\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\bm{Q}^{T}

be the spectral decomposition of
\bm{L}
, where
\bm{Q}
is orthogonal.
For any
\bm{\Sigma}=\bm{U}\,\mathrm{diag}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{d})\bm{U}^{T}

satisfying
c\leqslant y_{i}\leqslant C
,
the Hoffman–Wielandt inequality (and the unitary invariance of the Frobenius norm) gives

\|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})-\vect(\bm{L})\|^{2}\geqslant\sum_{i=1}^{d}(y_{i}-\lambda_{\pi(i)})^{2},

for some permutation
\pi
, with equality if and only if
\bm{U}=\bm{Q}
.
Hence the optimal choice is
\bm{U}=\bm{Q}
, and the minimization reduces to

d
independent scalar problems

\min_{c\leqslant y_{j}\leqslant C}(y_{j}-\lambda_{j})^{2},\qquad j=1,\ldots,d,

whose unique solutions are

y_{j}=\tilde{\lambda}_{j}=\min\{\max\{\lambda_{j},c\},C\}.

Therefore,

\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}}=\bm{Q}\bm{\Lambda}_{c}\bm{Q}^{T}.

Since both subproblems are strictly convex and are defined on closed convex sets (Lemma
1
), the solution

(\tilde{\bm{\mu}},\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}})
is unique.
Thus

\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(\bm{t})=(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\tilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{T})^{T}

is the metric projection of
\bm{t}
onto
\bm{\Theta}
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|
.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Then, for all

u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
,

\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\|^{2}\;\leqslant\;\big\langle\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v),\,u-v\big\rangle.

(A.21)

In particular,
\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}
is
1
-Lipschitz with respect to
\|\cdot\|
,
that is,

\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\|\;\leqslant\;\|u-v\|.

(A.22)

Proof.

The characterization of the metric projection in a Hilbert space states that

\big\langle u-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u),\,z-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\big\rangle\leqslant 0\quad\forall z\in\bm{\Theta},

and similarly for
v
.
Taking
z=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)
in the first inequality and

z=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)
in the second yields

\big\langle u-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\big\rangle\leqslant 0,

\big\langle v-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\big\rangle\leqslant 0.

Adding these two inequalities yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\;\geqslant\;\langle u-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\rangle+\langle v-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle u-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\rangle-\langle v-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle(u-v)-(\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)),\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle u-v,\,\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)\rangle-\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\|^{2}.

Rearranging yields (
A.21
).

Finally, applying Cauchy–Schwarz with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
,

\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\|^{2}\leqslant\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(v)\|\,\|u-v\|,

which implies (
A.22
).
∎

Lemma 4
.

Fix
\bm{\theta}^{*}\in\operatorname{int}(\bm{\Theta})
and

g\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
If

\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(\bm{\theta}^{*}+g)=\bm{\theta}^{*},

then
g=0
.

Proof.

Since
\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}
is the metric projection with respect to

\|\cdot\|
, the projection optimality condition states that for any

u\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
and
x\in\bm{\Theta}
, such that

x=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(u)
implies
\big\langle u-x,\;z-x\big\rangle\leqslant 0
, for each
z\in\bm{\Theta}
.
Apply this with
u=\bm{\theta}^{*}+g
and
x=\bm{\theta}^{*}
to obtain

\big\langle g,\;z-\bm{\theta}^{*}\big\rangle\leqslant 0

\forall\,z\in\bm{\Theta}
.
Since
\bm{\theta}^{*}\in\operatorname{int}(\bm{\Theta})
, there exists a
\delta>0

such that for every
h
with
\|h\|<\delta
we have

\bm{\theta}^{*}+h\in\bm{\Theta}
.
Fix any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
For any
t\in(0,\delta/\|v\|)
,

z=\bm{\theta}^{*}+tv\in\bm{\Theta},

and hence

\big\langle g,\;tv\big\rangle\leqslant 0.

By linearity of the inner product, this is

t\,\big\langle g,v\big\rangle\leqslant 0.

Since
t>0
, we obtain

\big\langle g,v\big\rangle\leqslant 0.

Repeating the argument with
-v
in place of
v
yields

\big\langle g,v\big\rangle\geqslant 0.

Therefore,

\big\langle g,v\big\rangle=0\qquad\forall\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}.

By positive definiteness of the inner product,
this implies
g=0
.
∎

Proposition 0

(Restated)
.

Assume that the following conditions hold:

(A1)

there exists a
\bm{\eta}_{0}\in\bm{H}
such that
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\eta}_{0}
.

(A2)

\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)
converges uniformly in probability to a
limit
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})
over
\bm{\Theta}
, that is,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

(A3)

\pi(\cdot,\bm{\eta}_{0}):\bm{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}

is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}
and for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}:\ \|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geqslant\varepsilon}\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>0.

Then any sequence
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
satisfying

\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\bigr\|\rightarrow_{p}0

is consistent for
\bm{\theta}_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof is obtained by verifying the conditions of Proposition 2.1 of

Newey and McFadden 1994
.
Let

\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})=\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\bigr\|^{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q(\bm{\theta})=\bigl\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|^{2}.

By assumption,

\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})=\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\bigr\|^{2}\rightarrow_{p}0.

Since
\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\geqslant 0
for all
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
, we have

0\leqslant\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})\leqslant\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}),

and therefore

\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\leqslant\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})+o_{p}(1).

Hence
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
is an approximate minimizer of
\widehat{Q}_{n}

in the sense of
Newey and McFadden 1994
.
Theorem 2.1 of
Newey and McFadden 1994
states that if
Q(\bm{\theta})
satisfies:

(i)

Q
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}
,

(ii)

Q
is uniquely minimized at
\bm{\theta}_{0}
,

(iii)

\bm{\Theta}
is compact,

(iv)

\widehat{Q}_{n}
converges uniformly in probability to
Q
, that is,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})\bigr|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

then
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\theta}_{0}
.

By assumption,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

and hence also

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

Since
\pi(\cdot,\bm{\eta}_{0})
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}
, the objective function

Q(\bm{\theta})=\bigl\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|^{2}

is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}
, which establishes condition (i).

Since
Q(\bm{\theta})=0
if and only if
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})=\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
and, by the
separation condition, this can occur only when
\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}
, it follows
that
Q
is uniquely minimized at
\bm{\theta}_{0}
, which establishes condition (ii).

By Lemma
1
,
\bm{\Theta}
is closed.
Moreover, note that for any

\bm{\theta}=(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})\in\bm{\Theta}
we have

\|\bm{\mu}\|\leqslant M
and, since

c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant C
,

\|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})\|=\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}(\bm{\Sigma})^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leqslant\sqrt{d}\,\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})\leqslant\sqrt{d}\,C.

Hence,

\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}=\|\bm{\mu}\|^{2}+\|\vect(\bm{\Sigma})\|^{2}\leqslant M^{2}+d\,C^{2},

which proves that
\bm{\Theta}
is bounded.
Therefore,
\bm{\Theta}
is closed and bounded, and by the Heine-Borel theorem, it is also compact, which proves (iii).

Consider the absolute value of the difference between

\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})
and
Q(\bm{\theta})
, that is,

\displaystyle\bigl|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})\bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\bigl\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\bigr\|^{2}-\bigl\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|^{2}\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\bigl\|\big[\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big]+\big[\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\big[\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\big]\bigr\|^{2}-\bigl\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr\|^{2}\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}+\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{\!T}\big(\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\big(\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\big)^{\!T}\big(\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{\!T}\big(\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\big)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}+\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\,\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|\,\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\,\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}+\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\|.

Since
\pi(\cdot,\bm{\eta}_{0})
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}
and
\bm{\Theta}
is compact,
it follows that

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|

is bounded.
Therefore, since

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

it follows in particular that, at
\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}
,

\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

Because the observed sample
(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{y}_{i})
is a random sample from

F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
, the observed auxiliary estimator
\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})

has the same distribution as
\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)
.
Consequently,

\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

and, thus,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\bigl|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})\bigr|\rightarrow_{p}0,

which establishes condition (iv).

∎

Proposition 0

(Restated)
.

Define
T_{n}(\bm{\theta})=\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})+\Big[\bm{\theta}-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\Big]
, and assume that the following conditions hold:

(B1)

The mapping
T_{n}

is uniformly
L
-Lipschitz with
L<1
, that is,

\|T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1})-T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{2})\|\leqslant L\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|\qquad\forall\,\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\bm{\Theta}.

(B2)

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\in\operatorname{int}(\bm{\Theta})
.

Then
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
is unique and the sequence
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(\ell)}
converges in norm to
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
with linear rate for every
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(0)}\in\bm{\Theta}
, that is

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(\ell)}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\|\leqslant L^{\ell}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(0)}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\|.

Proof.

Define
F(\bm{\theta}):=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(T_{n}(\bm{\theta}))
for
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
.
By Lemma
3
, the metric projection is nonexpansive, hence
for any
\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\bm{\Theta}
,

\|F(\bm{\theta}_{1})-F(\bm{\theta}_{2})\|=\|\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1}))-\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{2}))\|\leqslant\|T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1})-T_{n}(\bm{\theta}_{2})\|\leqslant L\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|.

Thus
F
is a contraction with constant
L<1
.
Since
\bm{\Theta}
is a closed subset of a Hilbert space
(Lemma
1
), it is complete. Therefore, by the
Banach fixed-point theorem, there exists a unique

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\in\bm{\Theta}
such that

F(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})=\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},

that is,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(T_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})).

Moreover, for every
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(0)}\in\bm{\Theta}
, the iterates

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(\ell)}=F(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(\ell-1)}),\qquad\ell=1,2,\dots,

converge to
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
with linear rate

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(\ell)}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\|\leqslant L^{\ell}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{(0)}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\|.

Finally, as
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\in\operatorname{int}(\bm{\Theta})
,
then from
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}=\Pi_{\bm{\Theta}}(T_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}))

Lemma
4

implies
T_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})=\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
.
∎

Proposition 9
.

Let the tuning parameter estimator
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}\in\bm{H}
, computed on the observed sample, satisfies
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, with
\bm{\eta}_{0}\in\interior(\bm{H})
.
Assume

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

(A.23)

and

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.24)

Assume that

\pi:\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
is continuous on

\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
and that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}:\ \|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geqslant\varepsilon}\|\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>0,

with
\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\interior(\bm{\Theta})
.
Moreover, assume that
\pi
is differentiable in
\bm{\theta}
and
\bm{\eta}
at

(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
with full-rank Jacobian

\bm{A}_{0}=\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})}
and
\bm{K}_{0}:=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}
.

Then,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n},\qquad\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

Using Proposition
2
, we have
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\theta}_{0}
.

Let

\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\bar{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

By assumption,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and in particular
\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

By a first-order Taylor expansion of
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,
we have

\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})+\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0})+\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0})+\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),

where

\bm{B}_{0}:=\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})},

and

\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Since
\pi
is differentiable at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
, the remainder
\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
satisfies

\frac{\|\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|}{\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\rightarrow(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

(A.25)

Define, for
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\neq(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,

f(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\frac{\|\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|}{\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|},\qquad f(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})=0.

Due to (
A.25
),
f
is continuous at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
and

f(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})=0
.
By considering the sequence
(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{p}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,
the continuous mapping theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
yields

f(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\frac{\|\bm{r}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\rightarrow_{p}0,

that is,

\bm{r}_{n}:=\bm{r}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=o_{p}\bigl(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\bigr).

(A.26)

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{r}_{n}-\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

Thus,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}))-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}),

Taking norms and using the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+CB\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\bm{r}_{n}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|,

with
C:=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{K}_{0})<\infty
and
B:=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{B}_{0})<\infty
by assumption and, where
\sigma_{\max}(\cdot)
extracts the maximum singular value.

Moreover, since
\bm{r}_{n}=o_{p}\bigl(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\bigr)
, by the
definition of
o_{p}(\cdot)
, this means, for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\Pr\Big(\|\bm{r}_{n}\|\leq\varepsilon\bigl(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\bigr)\Big)\rightarrow 1.

On the event

E_{n,\varepsilon}:=\bigl\{\|\bm{r}_{n}\|\leqslant\varepsilon(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|)\bigr\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+CB\,\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\varepsilon\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+C\varepsilon\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

Rearranging terms yields, on
E_{n,\varepsilon}
,

(1-C\varepsilon)\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C(B+\varepsilon)\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

(A.27)

Choose
\varepsilon>0
such that
C\varepsilon<1
. Dividing both sides of
(
A.27
) by
(1-C\varepsilon)
, we obtain on
E_{n,\varepsilon}

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\leq C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|,

(A.28)

for finite constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.
Therefore, for any
t>0
,

\{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>t\}\subseteq E_{n,\varepsilon}^{c}\;\cup\;\Bigl\{C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>t\Bigr\}.

This implies

\Pr(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>t)\leq\Pr(E_{n,\varepsilon}^{c})+\Pr\Bigl(C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>t\Bigr).

Since the bound holds for all
t>0
, it also holds for any deterministic
sequence
t=t_{n}>0
. In particular, since by assumption

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

it follows by evaluating at
\bm{\theta}_{0}
and using that
the observed sample is a random sample from
F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
that

\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Therefore, taking
t=Mn^{-1/2}
and using

\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\qquad\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

together with
\Pr(E_{n,\varepsilon}^{c})\rightarrow 0
, we conclude that

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.29)

Moreover, since
\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
and

\|\bm{r}_{n}\|=o_{p}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|)
, we conclude that

\bm{r}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Define the combined remainder term

\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}:=-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

By the triangle inequality,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|\leq C\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}\|+C\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

From above we have
\|\bm{r}_{n}\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
and

\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, hence

\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}\|\rightarrow_{p}0
and

\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\|\rightarrow_{p}0
. Therefore,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|\rightarrow_{p}0,

which is equivalent to

\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Note that

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}))+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}.

Add and subtract
\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
:

\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})=\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\bm{r}_{\eta,n},

where

\bm{r}_{\eta,n}:=\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Since
\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})
is differentiable at
\bm{\eta}_{0}
with derivative

\bm{B}_{0}
, by definition of differentiability we have

\frac{\big\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0})\big\|}{\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\;\rightarrow\;0\quad\text{as }\bm{\eta}\rightarrow\bm{\eta}_{0}.

Define

h(\bm{\eta}):=\frac{\big\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0})\big\|}{\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|},\qquad h(\bm{\eta}_{0}):=0.

Then
h
is continuous at
\bm{\eta}_{0}
and
h(\bm{\eta}_{0})=0
. Since

\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\eta}_{0}
, the continuous mapping theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
yields

h(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{p}0
. Therefore,

\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|=\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\,h(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=o_{p}(\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|).

As
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, it follows that

\bm{r}_{\eta,n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.
Thus,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{\,*},\qquad\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{\,*}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where

\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{\,*}:=\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}+\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{\eta,n}.

∎

Lemma 5
.

Let
\bm{\Theta}
and
\bm{H}
be compact, and let the tuning parameters
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
.
The observed sample
\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
consists of i.i.d. observations drawn from
F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
, where

\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\interior(\bm{\Theta})
.
Let
\{F_{\bm{\theta}}:\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}\}
, where

\bm{\theta}=(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}
, be a
d
-dimensional
parametric family such that there exist i.i.d. random vectors

\bm{u}_{1},\ldots,\bm{u}_{n}
defined on
\mathcal{U}
with known distribution
P_{U}
, not depending on
\bm{\theta}
,
with
\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}=G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,n
,
where
\E\|\bm{u}_{1}\|^{2}<\infty
. Assume moreover, that there exists a measurable function

m:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
with
\E[m(\bm{u}_{1})^{2}]<\infty

such that for all
\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\bm{\Theta}
and all

\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
,

\|G(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{u})-G(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{u})\|\leq m(\bm{u})\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|.

For each
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
, define

\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),

with population targets

\mu_{a}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\E[a(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})]
and

\mu_{b}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\E[b(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})]
,
where the function
a:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
and
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
. Assume that there exists
c>0
such that for all
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
,
\min_{j}|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c
.
Define

E_{n}:=\Big\{\inf_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\ \min_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant c\Big\},

and assume that there exists
\alpha>1/2
such that
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-\alpha})

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
The estimator
\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
is defined componentwise, for
j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
, as

\big(\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\big)_{j}:=\begin{cases}\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant\tilde{\delta},\\[6.0pt]
\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\tilde{\delta}},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|<\tilde{\delta},\end{cases}

with
0<\tilde{\delta}\leqslant c
.
Define

P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\mu_{a}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}).

Assume
a
is Lipschitz continuous, which means that for some
L_{a}<\infty
, and any
\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and

\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
,

\|a(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1})-a(\bm{x}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leq L_{a}\bigl(\|\bm{x}_{1}-\bm{x}_{2}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr).

An analogous Lipschitz condition holds for
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
.
Moreover, assume that there exists an
M<\infty
such that for all

\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and all
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
,

\|a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})\|\leqslant M
,

\|b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})\|\leqslant M
.
The tuning parameter estimator
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
, computed on the observed sample, satisfies
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.
Further define

\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n):=\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{P}^{(h)}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n),

where
\widehat{P}^{(h)}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
is
\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
computed on
H
random samples
\{\bm{x}^{(h)}_{1,\bm{\theta}},\ldots,\bm{x}^{(h)}_{n,\bm{\theta}}\}_{h=1}^{H}
, with law

F_{\bm{\theta}}
.
Assume that the number of Monte Carlo replications
H
satisfies

\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}=o(n^{-1/2})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Then

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\big\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\right\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

Let
\bm{\theta}_{i}=(\bm{\mu}_{i}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{i})^{T})^{T}
,
i=1,2
. Then, for any
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
,

\|G(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{u})-G(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{u})\|\leqslant m(\bm{u})\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|,

where

\E[m(U)^{2}]<\infty
for
U\sim P_{U}
.
Define for any
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
,
f_{\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}}(\bm{u}):=a\big(G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}),\bm{\eta}\big)
,
with components
f_{\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},j}(\bm{u})
,
j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
.
By the assumptions on
G
and
a
, for every
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
and
j
,

\displaystyle\bigl|f_{\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1},j}(\bm{u})-f_{\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2},j}(\bm{u})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1}}(\bm{u})-f_{\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2}}(\bm{u})\|

\displaystyle\leqslant L_{a}\bigl(\|G(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{u})-G(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{u})\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leqslant L_{a}\bigl(m(\bm{u})\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leqslant m_{G}(\bm{u})\,\bigl(\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|^{2}+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2},

where
m_{G}(\bm{u}):=L_{a}\sqrt{2}\,\max\{m(\bm{u}),1\}
.
Moreover,
|f_{\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},j}(\bm{u})|\leqslant M<\infty
for all
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},\bm{u})
.
Thus
\{f_{\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},j}:(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}\}
is a
Lipschitz-parametric class indexed by a compact, and thus bounded, subset of

\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2+r}
, with
\E[m_{G}(U)^{2}]<\infty
.
The boundedness assumption ensures that the class admits a finite (hence
square-integrable envelope).
By Example 19.7 in
Van der Vaart 2000
the bracketing entropy integral is finite,
and therefore this class is
P_{U}
-Donsker by Theorem 19.5.
Consequently, for each fixed

j
,

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|(\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))_{j}-(\mu_{a}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))_{j}|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.30)

and, thus,

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\|\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-\mu_{a}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.31)

An identical argument gives

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\|\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-\mu_{b}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.32)

On the event
E_{n}
, for each
j
and each
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
,
both pairs
\big((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)
,
and
