\big((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)

belong to the domain
\{(a,b):|b|\geqslant c\}
as by assumption
\inf_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\min_{j}|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c
.
The function
g
is continuously differentiable there with
gradient
\nabla g(a,b)=(1/b,-a/b^{2})
, which is uniformly bounded since

|b|\geqslant c
and
|a|\leqslant M
. Thus
g
is Lipschitz, thus, there exists an
L_{g}<\infty

such that

|g(a_{1},b_{1})-g(a_{2},b_{2})|\leqslant L_{g}\big(|a_{1}-a_{2}|+|b_{1}-b_{2}|\big).

(A.33)

Applying this to the points to

(a_{1},b_{1})=((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))
and

(a_{2},b_{2})=((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))
yields

\big|\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\leq L_{g}\Big(\big|(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|+\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\Big),

as
\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)=g(a_{1},b_{1})
and
P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=g(a_{2},b_{2})
on
E_{n}
.
Taking the supremum over
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
, we obtain on the event
E_{n}

Y_{n}\leq L_{g}Z_{n},

where

Y_{n}:=\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|,\quad Z_{n}:=\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big(\|\bar{a}_{n}-\mu_{a}\|+\|\bar{b}_{n}-\mu_{b}\|\big).

Thus, we have

\Pr\big(\{Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)\leq\Pr(L_{g}Z_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}).

By (
A.31
) and (
A.32
),
Z_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence, for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
M<\infty
and
N_{1}

such that for all
n\geqslant N_{1}
,

\Pr(L_{g}Z_{n}>Mn^{-1/2})\leqslant\varepsilon/2
.
Since by assumption
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})\rightarrow 0
, by definition of convergence there exists

N_{2}
such that for all
n\geqslant N_{2}
,

\Pr(E_{n}^{c})\leqslant\varepsilon/2
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\Pr\big(Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\big)

\displaystyle=\Pr\big(\{Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)+\Pr\big(\{Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\big(\{Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)+\Pr(E_{n}^{c}).

Therefore, for all
n\geqslant N:=\max(N_{1},N_{2})
,

\Pr\big(Y_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\big)\leq\varepsilon/2+\varepsilon/2=\varepsilon
.
Because
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we conclude that

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.34)

Thus,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0},n)\|+\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|.

(A.35)

By (
A.34
), the second term on the right hand side is
O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

By assumption, for any
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
, any
\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
, and any

\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
,

\|a(G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}),\bm{\eta}_{1})-a(G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}),\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leq L_{a}\,\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|,

and analogously,

\|b(G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}),\bm{\eta}_{1})-b(G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}),\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leq L_{b}\,\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Averaging over
i
preserves the Lipschitz constant, so

\|\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leqslant L_{a}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|,\qquad\|\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leqslant L_{b}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

This means that

\big|(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|\leqslant L_{a}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|,\qquad\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|\leqslant L_{b}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

On
E_{n}
, apply (
A.33
) to

(a_{1},b_{1})=((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1}))
and

(a_{2},b_{2})=((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2}))
,
we obtain

\big|\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)\big|\leq L_{g}\Big(\big|(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|+\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|\Big).

Thus, for each
j
,

\big|\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)\big|\leq L_{g}(L_{a}+L_{b})\,\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Since there are finitely many entries,
there exists a finite constant
C_{P}
such that for any
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta},\ \bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
,

\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)\|\leqslant C_{P}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Taking the supremum over
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
preserves the bound for any
\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)\|\leqslant C_{P}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Applying this with
\bm{\eta}_{1}=\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
and
\bm{\eta}_{2}=\bm{\eta}_{0}
gives on
E_{n}

W_{n}\leqslant C_{P}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|.

where

W_{n}:=\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0},n)\|.

Therefore, for any
M>0
,

\Pr\big(\{W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)\leq\Pr\big(C_{P}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>Mn^{-1/2}\big).

Since
\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
,
for every
\varepsilon>0
there exist
M<\infty
and
N_{1}
such that
for all
n\geqslant N_{1}
,

\Pr\big(C_{P}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>Mn^{-1/2}\big)\leqslant\varepsilon/2
.
Moreover, since
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})\rightarrow 0
, there exists
N_{2}
such that
for all
n\geqslant N_{2}
,

\Pr(E_{n}^{c})\leqslant\varepsilon/2
.
Finally,

\displaystyle\Pr\big(W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\big)

\displaystyle=\Pr\big(\{W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)+\Pr\big(\{W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\big(\{W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\}\cap E_{n}\big)+\Pr(E_{n}^{c}).

Hence, for all
n\geqslant N:=\max(N_{1},N_{2})
,

\Pr\big(W_{n}>Mn^{-1/2}\big)\leq\varepsilon/2+\varepsilon/2=\varepsilon
.
Because
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we conclude that

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0},n)\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(A.36)

By using (
A.36
)
in (
A.35
), it yields

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Next, for each replication
h
, define

\bar{a}^{(h)}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a(\bm{x}^{(h)}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\bar{b}^{(h)}_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b(\bm{x}^{(h)}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta}).

Moreover, let
P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n):=\E\!\left[\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\right]

denotes the finite-sample target.
We have for all
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
and all
h
,

|\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leqslant M/\tilde{\delta},\qquad\forall\,(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H},\ \forall\,h=1,\dots,H.

(A.37)

Moreover,
we have
|\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leq|(\bar{a}_{n}^{(h)})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|/\tilde{\delta}
.
Since
(\bar{a}_{n}^{(h)})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
is Lipschitz in
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})

with constant
L_{a}
, it follows that

|\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)|\leq\frac{L_{a}}{\tilde{\delta}}\bigl(\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr).

Therefore, letting
L:=L_{a}/\tilde{\delta}
, for all
h
,

|\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1},n)-\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2},n)|\leq L\bigl(\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr),\qquad(\bm{\theta}_{k},\bm{\eta}_{k})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}.

(A.38)

Since
P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)=\E[\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)]
,
the same Lipschitz bound holds for
P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
by Jensen’s inequality.
Let
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
be a finite subset of
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}

such that for every
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}

there exists a
(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta})\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
with
\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{\delta}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}^{\delta}\|\leqslant\delta
.
Such a set exists because
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
is compact, hence totally bounded,
and
\#\mathcal{N}_{\delta}\leqslant C_{0}\,\delta^{-(r+d+d(d+1)/2)}
for some
C_{0}<\infty
.
For any
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
, choose

(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta})\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}

with distance at most
\delta
.
By adding and subtracting

\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)

and

P_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)

and using the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|

\displaystyle\leq|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)|

\displaystyle\quad+|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)|

\displaystyle\quad+|P_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|.

By (
A.38
),

\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)

is
L
-Lipschitz, hence so is its average
\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
.
Therefore

|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)|\leqslant L\delta.

Since
P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)
is also
L
-Lipschitz,

|P_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leqslant L\delta.

Combining the bounds gives

|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leq|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta}^{\delta},\bm{\eta}^{\delta},n)|+2L\delta,

and therefore

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leq\max_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|+2L\delta.

(A.39)

For each fixed
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
,
the variables

\{\widehat{P}^{(h)}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\}_{h=1}^{H}

are i.i.d. and bounded by
[-B,B]
, with
B:=M/\tilde{\delta}
, by construction.
Hence Hoeffding’s inequality yields, for any
t>0

\Pr\!\left(|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|>t\right)\leq 2\exp\!\left(-\frac{Ht^{2}}{2B^{2}}\right).

Choose
\delta=H^{-1/2}
and define

t_{H}:=B\sqrt{\frac{(r+d+\frac{d(d+1)}{2}+2)\log H+2\log(2C_{0})}{H}}.

Then

2C_{0}\,\delta^{-(r+d+\frac{d(d+1)}{2})}\exp\!\left(-\frac{Ht_{H}^{2}}{2B^{2}}\right)=H^{-1},

so that

\Pr\!\left(\max_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\mathcal{N}_{H^{-1/2}}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|>t_{H}\right)\leq H^{-1}.

Using (
A.39
) with
\delta=H^{-1/2}
, we have the implication

\displaystyle\Big\{\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|>t_{H}+2L\,H^{-1/2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\subseteq\Big\{\max_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\mathcal{N}_{H^{-1/2}}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|>t_{H}\Big\}.

Therefore,

\Pr\!\left(\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|>t_{H}+2L\,H^{-1/2}\right)\leq H^{-1}.

Since
\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}=o(n^{-1/2})
, it follows
H\rightarrow\infty
, we have

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|=O_{p}\!\bigl(t_{H}+2L\,H^{-1/2}\bigr).

Now,

t_{H}=B\sqrt{\frac{(r+d+\frac{d(d+1)}{2}+2)\log H+2\log(2C_{0})}{H}}=O\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}\right),

and clearly
H^{-1/2}=O\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}\right)
for large
H
.
Therefore,

t_{H}+2L\,H^{-1/2}=O\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}\right),

and we conclude

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}|\bar{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|=O_{p}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}\right).

(A.40)

Recall that

P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)=\E\!\left[\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\right],\qquad P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=g\!\left((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right),

where
g(a,b)=a/b
.
On the event
E_{n}
, we have
|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|\geqslant c
and also

|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c
by assumption, hence both points

\big((\bar{a}_{n})_{j},(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}\big)
and
\big((\mu_{a})_{j},(\mu_{b})_{j}\big)

belong to the domain
\{(a,b):|b|\geqslant c\}
.
Since
g(a,b)=a/b
is twice continuously differentiable on the domain

\{|b|\geqslant c\}
and its Hessian is uniformly bounded there, a second-order
Taylor expansion of
g
around

\big((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)
gives,
for some point

\xi
on the line segment joining

\big((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)
, and

\big((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)
,
the expansion

\displaystyle g\!\left((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)

\displaystyle=g\!\left((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)+\left(\nabla g_{0}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)^{T}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}^{T}H(\xi)\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}.

where

\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),

and

\nabla g_{0}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\nabla g\!\left((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right),\qquad H(\xi):=\nabla^{2}g(\xi).

Let
A_{i}:=a_{j}(\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}},\bm{\eta})
. Then
\E[A_{i}]=(\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
and

\E\!\left[\big(\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big)^{2}\right]=\Var\!\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\Var(A_{1})\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\E[A_{1}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{M^{2}}{n},

(A.41)

where the last inequality follows from
|A_{1}|\leqslant M
, uniformly in
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
.
An identical argument applies to
\Delta_{b,n}
.
Multiplying the Taylor expansion by
I_{E_{n}}
and taking expectations yields

\displaystyle\E\!\Big[\Big(g\!\left((\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)-g\!\left((\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)\Big)I_{E_{n}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\nabla g_{0}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)^{T}\E\!\Big[\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}I_{E_{n}}\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\,\E\!\Big[\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}^{T}H(\xi)\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}I_{E_{n}}\Big].

Note that
\E[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})]=0
and
\E[\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})]=0
.
Hence

\E[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}}]=-\E[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}^{c}}],\qquad\E[\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}}]=-\E[\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}^{c}}].

By Cauchy–Schwarz and the variance bound (
A.41
),

\displaystyle\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big|\E[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}}]\big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big|\E[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})I_{E_{n}^{c}}]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\sqrt{\E\!\big[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{2}\big]}\,\sqrt{\Pr(E_{n}^{c})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\Pr(E_{n}^{c})}.

and similarly for
\Delta_{b,n}
.
Since
\|\nabla g_{0}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|
is uniformly bounded
(because
|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c
and
|(\mu_{a})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|\leqslant M
),
it follows that the linear term is

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\left|\left(\nabla g_{0}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right)^{T}\E\!\Big[\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}I_{E_{n}}\Big]\right|=O\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\Pr(E_{n}^{c})}\right).

On
E_{n}
, the point
\xi
lies in
\{|b|\geqslant c\}
, hence
\|H(\xi)\|\leqslant C
for a constant
C<\infty
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\left|\E\!\Big[\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}^{T}H(\xi)\begin{pmatrix}\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\\
\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\end{pmatrix}I_{E_{n}}\Big]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\,\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\E\!\Big[\big(\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{2}+\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{2}\big)I_{E_{n}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\,\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}\E\!\big[\Delta_{a,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{2}\big]+C\,\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}\E\!\big[\Delta_{b,n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{2}\big]=O(n^{-1}).

The last equality uses (
A.41
) and the analogous for
\Delta_{b,n}
.
Recall that

|\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)|\leqslant|(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|/\tilde{\delta}

and that

|P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|\leqslant\frac{M}{c}
.
Hence, using
|(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|\leqslant M
,

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\Big|\E\big[(\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big]\Big|\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}}+\frac{1}{c}\right)\Pr(E_{n}^{c}).

Using that for any integrable random vector
X
, we have

\E[X]=\E[XI_{E_{n}}]+\E[XI_{E_{n}^{c}}]
, then

\displaystyle P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle=\E\!\big[(\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))I_{E_{n}}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\E\!\big[(\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big].

Taking absolute values and the supremum over
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big|P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}\Big|\E\big[(\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))I_{E_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}\Big|\E\big[(\widehat{P}_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}))I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big]\Big|.

Combining the bounds above yields

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big|P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|=O(n^{-1})+O\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\Pr(E_{n}^{c})}\right)+O\!\big(\Pr(E_{n}^{c})\big).

By assumption,
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-\alpha})
, so

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\big|P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|=O(n^{-1})+O\!\big(n^{-(1+\alpha)/2}\big)+O(n^{-\alpha}).

Since
\alpha>1/2
then the right-hand side is
o(n^{-1/2})
.
Then,

\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\left\|P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right\|=o(n^{-1/2}).

(A.42)

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\right\|+\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)\right\|+\sup_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\left\|P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\right\|.

Combining (
A.40
) and (
A.42
) yields

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\right\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}\right)+o(n^{-1/2}).

By assumption
\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}=o(n^{-1/2})
, we conclude that

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\left\|\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\right\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

∎

Proposition 10
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
2
, further assume that for each
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
L>0
such that

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L\Big)>\varepsilon\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.43)

Then, for every
\varepsilon>0
,

{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\varepsilon)\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

Proof.

Set
\Delta_{n}^{*}:=\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|
. From (
A.43
), for each
\varepsilon>0
there exists a constant

L_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\Delta_{n}^{*}>L_{\varepsilon}\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.44)

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and an arbitrary
L>0
. Since
L>0
, we can choose

N_{1}
such that
\sqrt{n}\,L\geqslant L_{\varepsilon}
for all
n\geqslant N_{1}
.
For such
n
we have the inclusion of events

\{\Delta_{n}^{*}>L\}\subseteq\{\sqrt{n}\,\Delta_{n}^{*}>L_{\varepsilon}\}
,
which implies, for every fixed sample, that

{\Pr}^{*}(\Delta_{n}^{*}>L)\leq{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\Delta_{n}^{*}>L_{\varepsilon})
.
Therefore, for any
n\geqslant N_{1}

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\Delta_{n}^{*}>L)>\varepsilon\Big)\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\Delta_{n}^{*}>L_{\varepsilon})>\varepsilon\Big).

Since
L>0
,
\varepsilon>0
are arbitrary, this proves that the for every
L>0
and

\varepsilon>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L\Big)>\varepsilon\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.45)

Using the triangle inequality

\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leq\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|+\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|.

(A.46)

Hence, for
L>0
,

\Big\{\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L\Big\}\subseteq\Big\{\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L/2\Big\}\cup\Big\{\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L/2\Big\},

as (
A.46
)
implies that whenever the left-hand side exceeds
L
, at least one of the
two terms on the right must exceed
L/2
.
Taking bootstrap probability
{\Pr}^{*}
of both sides and then outer
probability
\Pr
, we obtain for any fixed
L>0
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L\Big)>\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Pr\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L/2\Big)+\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L/2\Big)>\varepsilon/2\Big).

The first term converges to zero by assumption, while the
second term converges to zero by (
A.45
) .
Therefore, for every
L>0
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L\Big)>\varepsilon\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.47)

Note that,
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}
satisfies

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}=\argmin_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta}),

where
\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})=\big\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)\big\|^{2}
.
Let
Q(\bm{\theta})=\|\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}
.
Repeating the same algebra as in the proof of
Proposition
2
, but replacing
\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
with
\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
we obtain,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|\;\leq\;C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}+C_{2}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2},

for suitable finite constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.
Now fix
\delta>0
.
Choose
L_{1}>0
and
L_{2}>0
such that
C_{1}L_{1}^{2}+C_{2}L_{2}^{2}<\delta
.
Consider the event

E_{n}=\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\delta\Big\}.

By the inequality above, if both

\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leqslant L_{1}

and

\sup_{\bm{\theta}}\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leqslant L_{2}
,
then

\sup_{\bm{\theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|\leqslant C_{1}L_{1}^{2}+C_{2}L_{2}^{2}<\delta
.
Taking complements yields

E_{n}\subseteq\Big\{\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{1}\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{2}\Big\}.

Taking
{\Pr}^{*}
of both sides

{\Pr}^{*}(E_{n})\leq{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{1}\Big)+\bm{1}\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{2}\Big\}.

Finally, taking
\Pr
of both sides and using
(
A.47
) for the first term and the fact that the assumptions implies
\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\overline{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0
(see the proof of Proposition
2
) for the second,
we obtain for any
\varepsilon>0

\Pr\big({\Pr}^{*}(E_{n})>\varepsilon\big)\rightarrow 0.

This proves that for any
\delta>0

{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\delta\Big)\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

(A.48)

From Proposition
2
, we know that
\bm{\Theta}
is compact,
Q
is continuous, and
Q(\bm{\theta})
is uniquely minimized at
\bm{\theta}_{0}
.
Let

A_{\delta}:=\{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geq\delta\}
, then by assumption, we have
\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in A_{\delta}}Q(\bm{\theta})>0
.
Fix

0<\eta\leq\Delta_{\delta}/3
and
\delta>0
, with
\Delta_{\delta}:=\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in A_{\delta}}Q(\bm{\theta})>0
.
Then

\inf_{\bm{\theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geq\delta}Q(\bm{\theta})\geqslant 3\eta.

(A.49)

Let
U:=\{\bm{\theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|<\delta\}
and
U^{c}:=\{\bm{\theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geq\delta\}
.
Consider the event

B_{n}=\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|\leqslant\eta\Big\}.

Then on
B_{n}
, we have that

\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta}_{0})\leqslant\eta.

and by (
A.49
), for
\bm{\theta}\in U^{c}
:

\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})\geq Q(\bm{\theta})-\eta\geq 3\eta-\eta=2\eta.

Thus, on
B_{n}
, and for any
\bm{\theta}\in U^{c}

\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta}_{0})\leqslant\eta\leqslant\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-\eta<\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta}).

On
B_{n}
,
\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta}_{0})
is strictly smaller than

\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})
for all
\bm{\theta}\in U^{c}
, hence the minimizer

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}
must belong to
U
.
Hence,

\{\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}\in U^{c}\}\subseteq\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\eta\Big\},

and taking conditional probability
{\Pr}^{*}
yields

{\Pr}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}\in U^{c})\;\leq\;{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\eta\Big).

Consequently, for any
t>0
,

\big\{{\Pr}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}\in U^{c})>t\big\}\subseteq\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\eta\Big)>t\Big\},

and hence by (
A.48
)

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}\in U^{c})>t\Big)\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}|\widehat{Q}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\theta})-Q(\bm{\theta})|>\eta\Big)>t\Big)\rightarrow 0.

This implies

{\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\delta)\rightarrow_{p}0.

∎

Proposition 11
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
9
hold and that for each
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
L>0
such that

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\big\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big\|>L\Big)>\varepsilon\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.50)

Then

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*},

where
{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0
.

Proof.

From Proposition
10
, we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,
{\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0
.
Let,

\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\bar{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

By assumption,

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and in particular
\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.
Then

X_{n}:=\sqrt{n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=o_{p}(1).

Fix
M>0
and
\varepsilon>0
, for large
n
, we have

\Pr(X_{n}>M)\leqslant\varepsilon.

(A.51)

Define the event
E_{n}:=\{X_{n}\leqslant M\}
. On
E_{n}
, since
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}\in\bm{\Theta}
,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leqslant\sqrt{n}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|=X_{n}\leqslant M,

hence

{\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>M\big)=0.

Therefore, for any
\delta>0
,

\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>M\big)>\delta\Big\}\subseteq\{X_{n}>M\}.

Taking
\Pr
on both sides and using (
A.51
) yields, for all
n\geqslant N
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>M\big)>\delta\Big)\leqslant\Pr(X_{n}>M)\leqslant\varepsilon.

Thus, for each
\delta,M,\varepsilon>0
we have for large
n

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*})\|>M\big)>\delta\Big)\leqslant\varepsilon.

(A.52)

By a first-order Taylor expansion of
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,
we have

\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})+\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0})+\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0})+\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),

where

\bm{B}_{0}:=\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})},

and

\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Since
\pi
is differentiable at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
, the remainder
\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
satisfies

\frac{\|\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|}{\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\rightarrow(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

(A.53)

Define, for
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\neq(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,

f(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\frac{\|\bm{r}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\|}{\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|},\qquad f(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})=0.

Due to (
A.53
),
f
is continuous at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
and

f(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})=0
.
Hence, for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
\eta>0
such that

\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\leqslant\eta\quad\text{implies}\quad f(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\leqslant\varepsilon.

(A.54)

From (
A.54
),

\big\{f(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})>\varepsilon\big\}\subseteq\big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big\},

and therefore

{\Pr}^{*}\!\big(f(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})>\varepsilon\big)\;\leq\;{\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big).

Let
\delta>0
. Taking probability
\Pr
on both sides yields

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\big(f(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})>\varepsilon\big)>\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big)>\delta\Big).

Fix
\eta>0
and
\delta>0
. By the union bound,

\big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big\}\subseteq\big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big\}\;\cup\;\big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big\}.

Hence,

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big)

\displaystyle\leq{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big)+{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big).

(A.55)

The second term is

{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big)=\bm{1}\Big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big\}.

Therefore, from (
A.55
),

{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big)\leq{\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big)+\bm{1}\Big\{\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big\}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big)>\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\big)>\tfrac{\delta}{2}\Big)+\Pr\Big(\bm{1}\{\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\}>\tfrac{\delta}{2}\Big).

(A.56)

The first term in (
A.56
) converges to
0
because

{\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\eta/2)\rightarrow_{p}0
.
The second term is bounded by

\Pr\Big(\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big),

which converges to
0
since
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=o_{p}(1)
.
Hence,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\eta\big)>\delta\Big)\rightarrow 0,

and consequently, for all
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\varepsilon\Big)>\delta\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.57)

where
\bm{r}_{n}^{*}:=\bm{r}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.

Since
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}
satisfies the bootstrap normal equations with the nuisance
estimate
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
,

\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)=0,

we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{\pi}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{A}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}),

where the last equality follows from the first-order Taylor expansion of

\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
at
(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
, evaluated at

(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.
Thus,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

With
C:=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{K}_{0})<\infty
and
B:=\sigma_{\max}(\bm{B}_{0})<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+CB\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

From (
A.57
), for each
\varepsilon,\eta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\big)\Big)>\eta\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.58)

Define the event

E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*}:=\Big\{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\varepsilon\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\big)\Big\}.

On
E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*}
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+CB\,\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+CB\,\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\varepsilon\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+C\varepsilon\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

Rearranging yields, on
E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*}
,

(1-C\varepsilon)\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\leq C\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C(B+\varepsilon)\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

(A.59)

Choose
\varepsilon>0
such that
C\varepsilon<1
. Dividing both sides of
(
A.59
) by
(1-C\varepsilon)
, we obtain on
E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*}

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\leq C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|,

for finite constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.
Therefore, for any
t>0
,

\{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>t\}\subseteq(E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c}\;\cup\;\Bigl\{C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>t\Bigr\}.

Hence,

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>t)

\displaystyle\leq{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c})+{\Pr}^{*}\!\Bigl(C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>t\Bigr).

Taking
\Pr
on both sides gives

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>t)>\eta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}((E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c})>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\!\Bigl(C_{1}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+

\displaystyle\qquad C_{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>t\Bigr)>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Bigg).

Now set
t=Mn^{-1/2}
and note that, by the triangle inequality, if

C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>M,

then at least one of the following events must occur:

C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>\tfrac{M}{3},\qquad C_{2}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{M}{3},\qquad C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{M}{3}.

Hence, for every
M>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\!\Bigl(C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+C_{2}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|+C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>M\Bigr)>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)>\tfrac{\eta}{6}\Bigg)+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(C_{2}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)>\tfrac{\eta}{6}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)>\tfrac{\eta}{6}\Bigg).

Then by using (
A.58
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M)>\eta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}((E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c})>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big)+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)>\tfrac{\eta}{6}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Pr\Big(C_{2}\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(C_{1}\sqrt{n}\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{M}{3}\Big)>\tfrac{\eta}{6}\Bigg).

(A.60)

Next, we obtain a bound for

\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
.
Assumption (
A.50
) implies that there exists an
L_{1}>0
such that for large
n

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L_{1}\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\leq\varepsilon.

Moreover, since
\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, there exists an

L_{2}>0
such that for large
n
,

\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{2}\Big)\leq\varepsilon.

By the triangle inequality,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leq\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|+\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|.

Hence,

\displaystyle\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{1}+L_{2}\big)>\varepsilon\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\subseteq\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L_{1}\big)>\varepsilon\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{2}\Big\}.

Taking
\Pr
of both sides and using the two bounds established earlier, for large
n
,

\Pr\Big(\Pr^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L_{1}+L_{2}\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\leq 2\varepsilon.

This impleis, for each
\varepsilon>0
, there is an
L>0
such that for large
n
,

\Pr\Big(\Pr^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>L\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\leqslant\varepsilon.

(A.61)

In (
A.60
), fix
\eta>0
and choose
M
large enough such that

\tfrac{M}{3C_{1}}\geqslant L_{\pi}
and
\tfrac{M}{3C_{1}}\geqslant L_{\zeta}
, where
L_{\pi}
is the
L
in
(
A.61
) with
\varepsilon=\eta/6
and
L_{\zeta}
is the
M
in
(
A.52
) with
\delta=\eta/6
and
\varepsilon=\eta/6
.
Moreover, since
\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, we can choose
M
large
enough so that, for all large
n
,

\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\tfrac{M}{3C_{2}}\Big)\leqslant\tfrac{\eta}{6}.

From (
A.60
), with this choice of
M
, for large
n
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M)>\eta\Big)\leqslant\varepsilon+\tfrac{\eta}{6}+\tfrac{\eta}{6}+\tfrac{\eta}{6}.

for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, since

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big((E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c}\big)>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0,

implies that for large
n
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big((E_{n,\varepsilon}^{*})^{c}\big)>\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Big)\leqslant\varepsilon.

As
\varepsilon
is arbitrary fix
\varepsilon=\tfrac{\eta}{2}
.
Thus, for every
\eta>0
, there exists an

M>0
such that, for large
n
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M\big)>\eta\Big)\leq\eta.

Moreover, fix arbitrary
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta>0
and set

\eta:=\min(\varepsilon,\delta)
. Let
M
be the corresponding
constant. Then, for large
n
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\leq\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M\big)>\eta\Big)\leq\eta\leq\delta.

Thus, for every
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta>0
there exists an
M>0
such that,
for large
n
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\leqslant\delta.

(A.62)

Recall that for all
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\varepsilon\Big)>\delta\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0,

(A.63)

and note that

\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|=\sqrt{n}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\big)\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}.

Let
\varepsilon,\eta>0
. Then

\big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big\}\subseteq\Big\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\big)>\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\Big\}.

(A.64)

Indeed, if both

\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\leqslant\eta

and

\sqrt{n}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|)\leqslant\varepsilon/\eta

held, then

\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|=\sqrt{n}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|)\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\eta=\varepsilon,

contradicting
\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon
.
We further decompose the second event as

\Big\{\sqrt{n}\big(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|\big)>\frac{\varepsilon}{\eta}\Big\}\subseteq\Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}\ \cup\ \Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big\}\subseteq\;

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}.

(A.65)

Taking
{\Pr}^{*}
of both sides yields

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)\leq\;

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big)

\displaystyle+{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)+{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big).

(A.66)

Note that,

{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)=\bm{1}\Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}.

Therefore, for each
t>0
, using that if
a+b+c>t
then at least one term exceeds
t/3
,

\displaystyle\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big\}\subseteq\;

\displaystyle\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\Big\{\bm{1}\Big\{\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big\}>\frac{t}{3}\Big\}.

(A.67)

Taking
\Pr
on both sides gives

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big)\leq\;

\displaystyle\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big).

(A.68)

Fix
\varepsilon,t,\gamma>0
.
First, by (
A.62
) there exists an
M_{1}>0
such that, for all large
n
,

\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M_{1}\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{3}.

Second, since
\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, there exists an
M_{2}>0
such that, for all large
n
,

\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>M_{2}\Big)\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{3}.

Let
M:=\max\{M_{1},M_{2}\}
and define
\eta:=\frac{\varepsilon}{2M}
, so that

\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}=M
.
Then, for all large
n
,

\displaystyle\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>M\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{3},

(A.69)

\displaystyle\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2\eta}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|>M\Big)\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{3}.

(A.70)

Finally, by (
A.63
), for the fixed
\eta>0
chosen above,

\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)\rightarrow 0,

and therefore for large
n
,

\Pr\Bigg({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|}{\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|}>\eta\Big)>\frac{t}{3}\Bigg)\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{3}.

Combining (
D
), (
A.69
),
(
A.70
), and the last display, we conclude that for large
n

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big)\leqslant\gamma.

Since
\gamma>0
is arbitrary, this shows that for every
\varepsilon,t>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.71)

Finally, rewrite the decomposition of
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}
as

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

Add and subtract
\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
, then we have

\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bm{B}_{0}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0})=\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})+\bm{r}_{\eta,n},

where
\bm{r}_{\eta,n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
as shown at the end of the proof of Proposition
9
.
Thus

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{\eta,n}-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

Define the combined bootstrap remainder (including the nuisance-centering term)

\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}:=\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{\eta,n}-\bm{K}_{0}\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{K}_{0}\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

Then the decomposition becomes

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}.

Set
C:=\|\bm{K}_{0}\|<\infty
. By the triangle inequality,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|\leq C\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|+C\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|+C\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. Then

\big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big\}\subseteq\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big\}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big\}.

Taking
{\Pr}^{*}
on both sides yields

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big)\leq\;

\displaystyle{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)+{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)+{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big).

Since
\bm{r}_{\eta,n}
is computed on the original sample, it is fixed in the bootstrap
world, hence

{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)=\bm{1}\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big\}.

Now fix any
t>0
. Using that if
a+b+c>t
with
a,b,c\geqslant 0
then at least one term
exceeds
t/3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\big)>t\Big\}\subseteq\;

\displaystyle\Big\{\bm{1}\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\}>\tfrac{t}{3}\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)>\tfrac{t}{3}\Big\}\cup\Big\{{\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)>\tfrac{t}{3}\Big\}.

Taking
\Pr
gives

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big)\leq\;

\displaystyle\Pr\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)>\tfrac{t}{3}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\zeta_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3C}\Big)>\tfrac{t}{3}\Big).

(A.72)

By assumption,
\bm{r}_{\eta,n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, hence

\Pr(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{\eta,n}\|>\varepsilon/(3C))\rightarrow 0
.
Moreover, by (
A.71
) and (
A.52
),
the last two terms on the right-hand side of (
A.72
)
converge to
0
. Therefore, for any
\varepsilon,t>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)>t\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0,

which is equivalent to
{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0
.
∎

Proposition 12
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
5
, let
\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
denote the observed sample, assumed to be randomly drawn
from
F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
.
For each tuning parameters
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
, define

\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}).

Denote the corresponding population targets by

\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta}):=\E[a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})]
, and
\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta}):=\E[b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})]
.
The estimator
\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\eta})
is defined componentwise, for

j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
, as

\big(\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\eta})\big)_{j}:=\begin{cases}\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})}{(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant\tilde{\delta},\\[6.0pt]
\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})}{\tilde{\delta}},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|<\tilde{\delta},\end{cases}

Moreover

P(\bm{\eta}):=\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})
.
Let
Z(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}):=(a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})^{T},b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})^{T})^{T}
,
\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}:=\Cov(Z(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0}))
, and

\bm{D}_{0}:=\Big[\diag\big(\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1},\;-\diag\!\left(\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\right)\Big].

Then

\sqrt{n}\Big(\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-P(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\Big)\ \rightarrow_{d}N\!\Big(0,\ \bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\Big).

Proof.

Define

\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta})
, and

\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}):=\left(\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta})^{T},\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})^{T}\right)^{T}
,
and let

\bar{\Delta}_{n}:=\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
, and

\bar{\Delta}:=\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
.

With these definitions, the decomposition

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)=\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)+\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}-\bar{\Delta}),

(A.73)

holds.
By boundedness of
a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
, all coordinates of

a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})
have finite second moments. Moreover, since the observations

\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
are i.i.d., the vectors

Z_{1}(\bm{\eta}_{0}),\ldots,Z_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
are i.i.d.
Hence, by the multivariate central limit theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
,

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}).

We now show that
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}-\bar{\Delta})=o_{p}(1)
.
Let
Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
indicates the
j
-th entry of
Z(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
.
Define the real-valued class

\mathcal{F}_{j}:=\bigl\{\bm{x}\mapsto Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}):\ \bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}\bigr\},

for
j\in\{1,\ldots,2(d+d(d+1)/2)\}
.
By uniform boundedness, there exists an
M<\infty
such that

|Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})|\leqslant M
for all
\bm{x}
and all
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
, hence

\mathcal{F}_{j}
admits a constant envelope.
Moreover, by Lipschitz continuity of
a
and
b
in
\bm{\eta}
uniformly in
\bm{x}
,
there exist
L_{j}<\infty
such that for all
\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
and all
\bm{x}
,

|Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{1})-Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{2})|\leqslant L_{j}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Thus
\mathcal{F}_{j}
is a bounded
Lipschitz class indexed by a compact, and thus bounded, subset of

\mathbb{R}^{r}
. By Example 19.7 of
Van der Vaart 2000
, the bracketing entropy integral is
finite, and therefore
\mathcal{F}_{j}
is
F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
-Donsker by Theorem 19.5 of

Van der Vaart 2000
.
Define the random and limit functions

f_{n,j}(\bm{x}):=Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}),\qquad f_{0,j}(\bm{x}):=Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Since
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}\in\bm{H}
, then
f_{n,j}\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
. Moreover

\E\!\left[f_{0,j}(X)^{2}\right]=\E\!\left[Z_{j}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\right]<\infty,

by the uniform boundedness assumption on
a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
. Furthermore, by the Lipschitz bound,

|f_{n,j}(\bm{x})-f_{0,j}(\bm{x})|=|Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-Z_{j}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})|\leqslant L_{j}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|,

hence

\int(f_{n,j}(X)-f_{0,j}(X))^{2}dF_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}(X)\leqslant L_{j}^{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|^{2}\rightarrow_{p}0,

since
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

By Lemma 19.24 of
Van der Vaart 2000
, for each fixed
j
,

\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}-\bar{\Delta})_{j}=\sqrt{n}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl[\bigl(Z_{j}(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-Z_{j}(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr)-((\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))_{j}-(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0}))_{j})\Bigr])\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

Thus
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}-\bar{\Delta})=o_{p}(1)
.

Applying Slutsky’s theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
to the decomposition in (
A.73
) yields

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}).

(A.74)

Define
g:\mathbb{R}^{2(d+d(d+1)/2)}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
by
g(z):=u\oslash v,

with
z=(u^{T},v^{T})^{T}
,
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
Noting that
\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})=\mu_{b}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})
, by assumption, for every
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
,

\min_{j}|\mu_{b,j}(\bm{\eta})|>c
so
g
is differentiable at
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.
On the event
E_{n}
we have

\min_{j}|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})|\geqslant c
since
(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
has the same distribution as
(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
, it follows that
g
is well-defined at
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
on
E_{n}
.
Therefore, on the event
E_{n}
, a Taylor expansion of
g
at
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
gives

g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))=g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))+Dg(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))+\bm{r}_{n,g},

(A.75)

where

Dg(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))=\big[\,\diag(\mu_{b}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))^{-1},\ -\,\diag(\mu_{a}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\oslash\mu_{b}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})^{2})\,\big].

The remainder term satisfies for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\Pr\!\left(E_{n}\ \cap\ \left\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}\|}{\|\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|}>\varepsilon\right\}\right)\ \rightarrow\ 0,

equivalently

I_{E_{n}}\,\|\bm{r}_{n,g}\|=o_{p}\!\left(\|\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\right).

This holds because on
E_{n}
the map
g
is differentiable at
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
and

\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{p}0
, by using similar argument as in Proposition
9
.
Since
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, then

I_{E_{n}}\bm{r}_{n,g}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

By assumption, there exists an
L<\infty
such that for all

\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}
and all
\bm{x}
,

\|a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{1})-a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leqslant L\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Thus, by Jensen’s inequality

\|\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta}_{1})-\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta}_{2})\|=\big\|\E[a(X,\bm{\eta}_{1})-a(X,\bm{\eta}_{2})]\big\|\leq\E\|a(X,\bm{\eta}_{1})-a(X,\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leq L\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

An analogous result holds for
\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})
.
Hence each coordinate of
\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta})
and
\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})
is Lipschitz continuous in
\bm{\eta}
, and therefore continuous at
\bm{\eta}_{0}
.
Since
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\eta}_{0}
, the continuous mapping theorem

(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
implies
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{p}\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
.
Moreover, as
Dg(\cdot)
is continuous at
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
,
we obtain
Dg(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\rightarrow_{p}Dg(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0}))
.

Recall that
\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))
on
E_{n}
, hence

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-P(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)=I_{E_{n}}\,\sqrt{n}\big(g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big)+\sqrt{n}\,I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big(\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big).

Since
\|\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leqslant M/\tilde{\delta}
as
\|\bar{a}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leqslant M
and

\|g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\|\leqslant M/c
, we have

\Big\|\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\Big\|\leq\|\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|+\|g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\|\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}}+\frac{1}{c}\right).

Hence

\sqrt{n}\,I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big(P_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big)=O_{p}\!\big(\sqrt{n}\,I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big).

Since
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-\alpha})
with
\alpha>1/2
, we have that

\sqrt{n}\,I_{E_{n}^{c}}\big(P_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big)=o_{p}(1).

Moreover, because
\Pr(E_{n})\rightarrow 1
,
I_{E_{n}}\rightarrow_{p}1
, and therefore

I_{E_{n}}\,\sqrt{n}\big(g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}N\!\Big(0,\ \bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\Big),

by (
A.74
), (
A.75
), and Slutsky’s theorem

(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
. Therefore,

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-P(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N\!\Big(0,\ \bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\Big).

(A.76)

∎

Proposition 13
.

Under the same assuptions and notation of Proposition
12
, let
\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}^{*}
be a bootstrap sample from the empirical distribution function
F_{n}
from the observed sample
\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
, and set

\bar{a}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a(\bm{x}_{i}^{*},\bm{\eta}),\qquad\bar{b}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b(\bm{x}_{i}^{*},\bm{\eta}).

The estimator
\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})
is defined componentwise, for
j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
, as

\big(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})\big)_{j}:=\begin{cases}\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})}{(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant\tilde{\delta},\\[6.0pt]
\dfrac{(\bar{a}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})}{\tilde{\delta}},&\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|<\tilde{\delta}.\end{cases}

Then

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N\!\big(0,\,\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\big),

in probability.

Proof.

Define

\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta})
, and

\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}):=\left(\mu_{a}(\bm{\eta})^{T},\mu_{b}(\bm{\eta})^{T}\right)^{T}
,
and let

\bar{\Delta}_{n}:=\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
, and

\bar{\Delta}:=\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
, and
\Delta_{i}:=Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})
,

Define the bootstrap analogues
\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})
,
\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}
, and

\Delta_{i}^{*}
of
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta})
,
\bar{\Delta}_{n}
, and
\Delta_{i}
,
respectively, obtained by replacing each observation
\bm{x}_{i}
with its
bootstrap counterpart
\bm{x}_{i}^{*}
.
Then

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)=\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)+\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}).

(A.77)

By boundedness of
a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
, all coordinates of

a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})
have finite second moments.
Hence, by
Bickel and Freedman 1981
Theorem 2.2(a), we have

\mathcal{\!}\sqrt{n}(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0}))\rightarrow_{d}N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}),

(A.78)

holds almost surely.
Since

\Delta_{i}^{*}
are i.i.d. from the law assigning
mass
1/n
to each
\Delta_{i}
, then

\E^{*}(\Delta_{i}^{*})=\bar{\Delta}_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Cov^{*}(\Delta_{i}^{*})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Delta_{i}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})(\Delta_{i}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})^{T}.

Hence,

\Cov^{*}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*})=\Cov^{*}\!\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{*}\right)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Cov^{*}(\Delta_{i}^{*})=\frac{1}{n}\Cov^{*}(\Delta_{i}^{*}).

Then, it follows that

\Tr[\Cov^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})\big)]=n\,\Tr[\Cov^{*}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*})]=\Tr[\Cov^{*}(\Delta_{i}^{*})]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}\|^{2}.

Moreover, using
\bar{\Delta}_{n}=\E^{*}\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\E^{*}\Big\|\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Tr\!\Big(\Cov^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}\|^{2}.

Furthermore, by the inequality

\|u-v\|^{2}\leqslant 2\|u\|^{2}+2\|v\|^{2}

and Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}\|^{2}+2\|\bar{\Delta}_{n}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}\|^{2}=\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}\|^{2}.

By the Lipschitz assumptions on
a
and
b
, there exist constants
L_{a},L_{b}<\infty
such that, for every
i
,

\|\Delta_{i}\|\leq\|a(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-a(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+\|b(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-b(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leq(L_{a}+L_{b})\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|.

Hence,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Delta_{i}\|^{2}\leq(L_{a}+L_{b})^{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|^{2},

and therefore

\E^{*}\Big\|\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n})\Big\|^{2}\leq 4(L_{a}+L_{b})^{2}\|\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}\|^{2}\rightarrow_{p}0.

(A.79)

Let

T_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big),\qquad R_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}\big),

so by (
A.77
),

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)=T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*}.

Fix
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
, where
\bm{\Phi}:=\{\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{2(d+d(d+1)/2)}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|\leqslant L\|x-y\|,|\varphi(x)|\leqslant B,\quad x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2(d+d(d+1)/2)},L<\infty,B<\infty\}
is a set of all
bounded
L
-Lipschitz functions, then

\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\Big|\leq\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]\Big|+\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\Big|,

(A.80)

where
Z\sim N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab})
.
By linearity,

\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]\Big|=\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big]\Big|.

By Jensen’s inequality,

\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big]\Big|\leqslant\E^{*}\!\big|\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big|.

As,
\varphi
is Lipschitz,

\big|\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big|\leqslant L\|R_{n}^{*}\|.

Taking conditional expectations, we have

\E^{*}\!\big|\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big|\leqslant L\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\E^{*}\big[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big]\Big|\leqslant\E^{*}\big|\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})-\varphi(T_{n}^{*})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant L\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant L\sqrt{\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}},

(A.81)

where the last inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
By (
A.79
),
\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}\rightarrow_{p}0
, thus,
for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\Pr\!\big(\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}>\varepsilon\big)\ \rightarrow\ 0.

(A.82)

Moreover, by (
A.78
),
T_{n}^{*}\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}),

almost surely, and, thus, by the Portmanteau theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
, as
\varphi
is bounded and
L
-Lipschitz, we have

\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]\ \rightarrow_{p}\ \E[\varphi(Z)].

(A.83)

Combining (
A.80
) with the Boole’s inequality and using (
D
), (
A.82
), and (
A.83
), for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr(\Big|

\displaystyle\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\Big|>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\!\Big(\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]\Big|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big)+\Pr\!\Big(\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\Big|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\!\Big(\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}>\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2L}\right)^{2}\Big)+\Pr\!\Big(\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\Big|>\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big)\ \rightarrow\ 0.

We implicitly assume
L>0
as in the case
L=0
the bound holds trivially.
Therefore, for every bounded
L
-Lipschitz function
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
, and any
\varepsilon>0

\Pr\!\Big(\big|\E^{*}[\varphi(T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z)]\big|>\varepsilon\Big)\ \rightarrow\ 0,

which implies

\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}),

(A.84)

in probability.

Define

E_{n}^{*}:=\Big\{\inf_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\ \min_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant c\Big\},

Define

G_{n}:=E_{n}\cap E_{n}^{*}
.
On the event
G_{n}
, all denominators of
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
and
\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})

are bounded away from
0
by
c
.
Define
g:\mathbb{R}^{2(d+d(d+1)/2)}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
by

g(z)=u\oslash v
, where
z=(u^{T},v^{T})^{T}
and

u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
On
G_{n}
, the denominator blocks of both
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
and

\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
satisfy

\min_{j}|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})|\geqslant c
, and
\min_{j}|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})|\geqslant c
,
so
g
is well defined and differentiable at both

\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
and
\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.
Hence, on
G_{n}
, a first-order Taylor expansion of
g
at
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
gives

g(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))=Dg(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*},

(A.85)

where

\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}:=g(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-Dg(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big).

For similar arguments as in the proof of Proposition
11

to obtain (
A.57
), for each
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\Big\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|}>\varepsilon\Big\}\cap G_{n}\big)>\delta\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.86)

Moreover, as shown at the end of the proof of Proposition
12
,
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{p}\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
and
\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\mu_{Z}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, thus, as
Dg(\cdot)
is continuous at
\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0})
, the continuous mapping theorem

(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
implies

Dg(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))\rightarrow_{p}Dg(\mu_{Z}(\bm{\eta}_{0}))=\bm{D}_{0}.

(A.87)

Write

X_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))
and

Y_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}\,\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}
, with

\bm{D}_{n}:=Dg(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))
.
Fix a bounded
L
-Lipschitz function
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
.
By using the triangle inequality and splitting according to
G_{n}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|+\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\,I_{G_{n}^{c}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(g(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|+C_{\varphi}\,{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c}),

where
C_{\varphi}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\|\varphi(x)\|
, and
Z_{P}\sim N\!\big(0,\,\bm{D}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\big)
.
By using that the Taylor expansion holds on
G_{n}
and adding and
subtracting
\E^{*}[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})I_{G_{n}}]
yields
