\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(g(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))-g(\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}))))I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|.

(A.88)

In the last term in (
A.88
), add and subtract

\E^{*}[\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})]

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\varphi}{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c})

(A.89)

Combining (
A.88
) and (
D
) gives

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|+2\,C_{\varphi}\,{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c}).

(A.90)

From the definition of
L
-Lipschitz function and Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E^{*}\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\E^{*}\!\Big[\big|\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\big|\,I_{G_{n}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq L\,\E^{*}\!\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\,I_{G_{n}}\big].

(A.91)

From (
A.84
),
for every bounded Lipschitz function
\varphi
,

\E^{*}\!\left[\varphi(X_{n}^{*})\right]\;\rightarrow_{p}\;\E\!\left[\varphi(Z)\right],

(A.92)

where
Z\sim N(0,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab})
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
.
Since the Gaussian law is tight, there exists an
M<\infty
such that

\Pr(\|Z\|>M/2)<\varepsilon/4.

Define the bounded Lipschitz function
\phi_{M}:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow[0,1]
by

\phi_{M}(x)=\begin{cases}0,&\|x\|\leqslant M/2,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\|x\|-M/2}{M/2},&M/2<\|x\|<M,\\[6.0pt]
1,&\|x\|\geqslant M,\end{cases}

so that
I_{\{\|x\|>M\}}\;\leq\;\phi_{M}(x)\;\leq\;I_{\{\|x\|>M/2\}}
.
From the definition of
\phi_{M}
we have

I_{\{\|x\|>M\}}\;\leq\;\phi_{M}(x)
,
and therefore, taking conditional expectations,

{\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\|X_{n}^{*}\|>M\bigr)=\E^{*}\!\left[I_{\{\|X_{n}^{*}\|>M\}}\right]\;\leq\;\E^{*}\!\left[\phi_{M}(X_{n}^{*})\right].

(A.93)

As
\phi_{M}(x)\;\leq\;I_{\{\|x\|>M/2\}}
,
taking expectations with respect to
Z
gives

\E[\phi_{M}(Z)]\;\leq\;\E\!\left[I_{\{\|Z\|>M/2\}}\right]=\Pr(\|Z\|>M/2).

By (
A.92
) applied to
\phi_{M}
,

\E^{*}[\phi_{M}(X_{n}^{*})]\rightarrow_{p}\E[\phi_{M}(Z)]\leqslant\Pr(\|Z\|>M/2)<\varepsilon/4.

Since
\E^{*}[\phi_{M}(X_{n}^{*})]\to_{p}\E[\phi_{M}(Z)]<\varepsilon/4<\varepsilon
,
it follows that

\Pr\!\left(\E^{*}[\phi_{M}(X_{n}^{*})]>\varepsilon\right)\rightarrow 0.

Thus, by using (
A.93
), we have

\Pr\!\left({\Pr}^{*}(\|X_{n}^{*}\|>M)>\varepsilon\right)\;\leq\;\Pr\!\left(\E^{*}[\phi_{M}(X_{n}^{*})]>\varepsilon\right)\;\rightarrow\;0.

This shows that (
A.84
) implies that for every
\varepsilon>0

there exists an
M<\infty
such that

\Pr\!\left({\Pr}^{*}(\|X_{n}^{*}\|>M)>\varepsilon\right)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.94)

Define
\Delta_{n}^{*}:=\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.
Then, from (
A.86
), for every
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,

\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\Big\{\tfrac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|}>\varepsilon\Big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)>\delta\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0.

(A.95)

Moreover, (
A.94
) implies that for every
\varepsilon,\delta>0

there exists an
L<\infty
such that for large
n

\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|>L\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\;<\;\delta.

(A.96)

Fix
\delta^{\prime}>0
.
For any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, consider the events

A_{n}:=\Big\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|}\leqslant\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n},\qquad A_{n}^{c}:=\Big\{\frac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|}>\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n}.

On
A_{n}
we have

\|Y_{n}^{*}\|=\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|\leqslant\varepsilon^{\prime}\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|.

Therefore, whenever both
A_{n}
and

\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta^{\prime}/\varepsilon^{\prime}\}
occur, we obtain

\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\delta^{\prime}.

Equivalently,

\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\;\subseteq\;A_{n}^{c}\;\cup\;\Big\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}/\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big\}.

Applying
{\Pr}^{*}
and using the union bound gives

{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\Big\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)\;\leq\;{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\Big\{\tfrac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|}>\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)\;+\;{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}/\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big).

(A.97)

From (
A.97
), for
\eta>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\Big\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)>\eta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\Big\{\tfrac{\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|}{\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|}>\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)>\eta/2\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}/\varepsilon^{\prime}\Big)>\eta/2\Big).

The first probability tends to zero by (
A.95
).
For the second probability, choose
\varepsilon^{\prime}
such that

\delta^{\prime}/\varepsilon^{\prime}>L
, where
L
is from (
A.96
).
Then by (
A.96
) the second probability also tends to zero.
Thus for any
\delta^{\prime},\eta>0
,

\Pr\!\Big({\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\big\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)>\eta\Big)\;\rightarrow\;0,

that is,

{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\big\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\big\}\cap G_{n}\Big)\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

(A.98)

Fix
\delta^{\prime}>0
. Write

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{G_{n}}\big]=\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}}\big]+\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}}\big].

On
\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}
we have
\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\delta^{\prime}
, hence

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}}\big]\leq\delta^{\prime}.

Moreover, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}}\big]\leq\sqrt{\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\big]}\;\sqrt{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}\big)}.

Therefore,

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{G_{n}}\big]\leq\delta^{\prime}+\sqrt{\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\big]}\;\sqrt{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}\big)}.

(A.99)

By (
A.98
), for every fixed
\delta^{\prime}>0
,

{\Pr}^{*}\big(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}\big)\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0,

and hence

\sqrt{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}\big)}\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

Moreover, on
G_{n}
the map
g(u,v)=u\oslash v
has uniformly bounded second
derivatives, so there
exists a constant
K<\infty
such that on
G_{n}
,

\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|\leqslant K\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2},\qquad\Delta_{n}^{*}:=\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}).

Therefore, on
G_{n}
,

\|Y_{n}^{*}\|=\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n,g}^{*}\|\leq K\sqrt{n}\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2},

and thus

\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\leq K^{2}\,n\,\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}.

Taking conditional expectations yields

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\big]\leq K^{2}\,n\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}.

(A.100)

By assumption
\|\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leqslant M
and
\|\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leqslant M
, and hence

\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|=\|\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|\leq 2M,\qquad\text{so}\qquad\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}\leqslant(2M)^{2}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}.

Therefore,

\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}\leq(2M)^{2}\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}.

(A.101)

Since
\Delta_{i}^{*}:=Z(\bm{x}_{i}^{*},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
are i.i.d. under
{\Pr}^{*}

with
\E^{*}[\Delta_{i}^{*}]=0
and
\|\Delta_{i}^{*}\|\leqslant 2M
, we have

\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}=\E^{*}\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{*}\Big\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\E^{*}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{*}\Big\|^{2}.

Expanding the square and using independence and
\E^{*}\Delta_{i}^{*}=0
yields

\E^{*}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{*}\Big\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E^{*}\|\Delta_{i}^{*}\|^{2}+2\sum_{i<j}\E^{*}\big[(\Delta_{i}^{*})^{T}\Delta_{j}^{*}\big]=n\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{1}^{*}\|^{2}.

Therefore,

\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{1}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}(2M)^{2}.

Combining this bound with (
A.101
) yields

\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}\leq(2M)^{2}\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}\leq(2M)^{2}\cdot\frac{(2M)^{2}}{n}=\frac{16M^{4}}{n},

and hence

n\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}\leqslant 16M^{4}.

Substituting into (
A.100
) gives

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\big]\leq K^{2}\,n\,\E^{*}\|\Delta_{n}^{*}\|^{4}\leq 16K^{2}M^{4}.

In particular,
\E^{*}[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}]=O_{p}(1)
, and thus

\sqrt{\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|^{2}I_{G_{n}}\big]}\;\sqrt{{\Pr}^{*}\big(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n}\big)}\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

Substituting this and
{\Pr}^{*}(\{\|Y_{n}^{*}\|>\delta^{\prime}\}\cap G_{n})\to_{p}0
into
(
A.99
), and letting
\delta^{\prime}\downarrow 0
, we obtain

\E^{*}\big[\|Y_{n}^{*}\|I_{G_{n}}\big]\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

Thus, from (
A.91
), we obtain

\Big|\E^{*}\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E^{*}\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

(A.102)

It remains to control the second term in (
A.90
), namely

\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|.

On
G_{n}
, for any
x
, by the
L
-Lipschitz property of
\varphi
,

|\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}x)-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}x)|\leq L\,\|(\bm{D}_{n}-\bm{D}_{0})x\|\leq L\,\|\bm{D}_{n}-\bm{D}_{0}\|\,\|x\|\leq L\,\|\vect(\bm{D}_{n})-\vect(\bm{D}_{0})\|\,\|x\|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\E^{*}\!\Big[|\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})|\,I_{G_{n}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq L\,\|\vect(\bm{D}_{n})-\vect(\bm{D}_{0})\|\;\E^{*}\!\big[\|X_{n}^{*}\|\,I_{G_{n}}\big].

(A.103)

Recall the decomposition

X_{n}^{*}=T_{n}^{*}+R_{n}^{*},\qquad T_{n}^{*}=\sqrt{n}\big(\bar{Z}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big),\qquad R_{n}^{*}=\sqrt{n}(\bar{\Delta}_{n}^{*}-\bar{\Delta}_{n}).

By the triangle inequality,

\E^{*}\|X_{n}^{*}\|\leq\E^{*}\|T_{n}^{*}\|+\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|.

(A.104)

Since
a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
and
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
are uniformly bounded, there exists an
M>0
such that

\|Z(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leq\|a(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|+\|b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|\leqslant 2M.

Conditionally on the data,
Z(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0}),\ldots,Z(\bm{x}_{n}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0})
are i.i.d. with

{\Pr}^{*}\big(Z(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0})=Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)=1/n
.
Hence
\E^{*}[T_{n}^{*}]=0
and by similar reasoning as in the proof of (
A.79
), we have

\displaystyle\E^{*}\|T_{n}^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Tr\!\big(\Cov^{*}(T_{n}^{*})\big)=\Tr\!\big(\Cov^{*}(Z(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0}))\big)

\displaystyle=\E^{*}\big\|Z(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\E^{*}(Z(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}_{0}))\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\bar{Z}_{n}(\bm{\eta}_{0})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|Z(\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|^{2}\leqslant(2M)^{2}.

Hence, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\E^{*}\|T_{n}^{*}\|\leq\sqrt{\E^{*}\|T_{n}^{*}\|^{2}}\leq 2M.

Moreover, by (
A.79
),

\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}\to_{p}0
, and therefore

\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|\leq\sqrt{\E^{*}\|R_{n}^{*}\|^{2}}\to_{p}0.

Combining these bounds in (
A.104
) yields

\E^{*}\|X_{n}^{*}\|\leq 2M+o_{p}(1),

so that
\E^{*}\|X_{n}^{*}\|=O_{p}(1)
.
Moreover, since
I_{G_{n}}\leqslant 1
, we have

\E^{*}\!\big[\|X_{n}^{*}\|\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\leqslant\E^{*}\|X_{n}^{*}\|
.
By (
A.87
),

\|\vect(\bm{D}_{n})-\vect(\bm{D}_{0})\|\to_{p}0
.
Therefore, by (
A.103
),

\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

(A.105)

By (
A.84
) and using the same reasoning as in the proof of Proposition
14
to obtain (
A.113
),
\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}\rightarrow_{d}Z_{P}
in probability, and as the map
\varphi
is bounded Lipschitz, we have

\Big|\E^{*}[\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})]-\E[\varphi(Z_{P})]\Big|\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

(A.106)

Recall that
G_{n}=E_{n}\cap E_{n}^{*}
. Then

G_{n}^{c}\subseteq E_{n}^{c}\cup(E_{n}^{*})^{c}
,
hence

{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c})\leq{\Pr}^{*}(E_{n}^{c})+{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n}^{*})^{c}).

Since
E_{n}
depends only on the original sample,
{\Pr}^{*}(E_{n}^{c})=I_{E_{n}^{c}}
.
By assumption,
{\Pr}(E_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-\alpha})
with
\alpha>1/2
, hence

I_{E_{n}^{c}}\to_{p}0
, and therefore

{\Pr}^{*}(E_{n}^{c})\to_{p}0
.

It remains to show that
{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n}^{*})^{c})\to_{p}0
.
Fix
\gamma>0
and define the buffered event

E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}:=\Big\{\inf_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\min_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant c+\gamma\Big\}.

On the event
E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}
, if
(E_{n}^{*})^{c}
occurs, then there exist

\tilde{\bm{\eta}}\in\bm{H}
and
j
such that
|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})|<c
and

|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})|\geqslant c+\gamma
. Hence,

\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})\big|\geq\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})\big|-\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\tilde{\bm{\eta}})\big|>(c+\gamma)-c=\gamma,

and therefore

(E_{n}^{*})^{c}\cap E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}\subseteq\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma\Big\}.

Consequently,

{\Pr}^{*}\!\big((E_{n}^{*})^{c}\cap E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}\big)\leq{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma\Big).

(A.107)

Let
\rho:=\gamma/(4L_{b})
. Since
\bm{H}
is compact, hence totally bounded, there exists a finite
\rho
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\rho}\subset\bm{H}
,
that is, for every
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
there exists
\bm{\eta}^{\rho}\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}
with

\|\bm{\eta}-\bm{\eta}^{\rho}\|\leq\rho
, and
\#\mathcal{N}_{\rho}<\infty
.
Since
b(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
is
L_{b}
-Lipschitz in
\bm{\eta}
, both

\bar{b}_{n}(\bm{\eta})
and
\bar{b}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\eta})
inherit the same Lipschitz constant
L_{b}
, that is, for all
\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
,

\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|\leq L_{b}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|,

and

\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}_{1})-(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}_{2})\big|\leq L_{b}\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|.

Then, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|.

By Lipschitz continuity,

\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})\big|\leqslant L_{b}\rho,\qquad\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\leqslant L_{b}\rho,

hence

\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\leq\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})\big|+2L_{b}\rho.

As
\rho=\gamma/(4L_{b})
, we have
2L_{b}\rho=\gamma/2
, and therefore

\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\leq\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta}^{\rho})\big|+\gamma/2.

Hence,

\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma\Big\}\subseteq\Big\{\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma/2\Big\}.

Fix
\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}
and
j
. Conditionally on the data,

b_{j}(\bm{x}_{1}^{*},\bm{\eta}),\dots,b_{j}(\bm{x}_{n}^{*},\bm{\eta})
are i.i.d. bounded in
[-M,M]

with mean
(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})
, and

(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{j}(\bm{x}_{i}^{*},\bm{\eta})
. Therefore, by conditional Hoeffding’s inequality,

{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma/2\Big)\leq 2\exp\!\left(-\frac{n\gamma^{2}}{8M^{2}}\right).

A union bound over
j
and
\mathcal{N}_{\rho}
yields

{\Pr}^{*}\!\Big(\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\rho}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n}^{*})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|>\gamma/2\Big)\leq 2\big(d+d(d+1)/2\big)\,\#\mathcal{N}_{\rho}\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{n\gamma^{2}}{8M^{2}}\right)\rightarrow 0.

Combining with (
A.107
) gives

{\Pr}^{*}\!\big((E_{n}^{*})^{c}\cap E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}\big)\rightarrow 0,

almost surely.

Define
c_{0}:=\inf_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\min_{j}|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\eta})|
.
By assumption,
c_{0}>c
. Fix
\gamma>0
such that
c+\gamma<c_{0}
, and set

\varepsilon:=c_{0}-(c+\gamma)>0
.
Recall that

(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})=(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})
, then by (
A.32
), we have

\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|\to_{p}\;0.

Hence,

\Pr\!\left(\sup_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\max_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})-(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\eta})\big|<\varepsilon\right)\rightarrow 1.

On this event, for every
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
and every
j
,

|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})|\geq|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\eta})|-\varepsilon\geq c_{0}-\varepsilon=c+\gamma,

and therefore

\inf_{\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}}\min_{j}|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\eta})|\geq c+\gamma,

that is,
E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}
occurs. Consequently,

\Pr(E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)})\rightarrow 1
.
Finally,

{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n}^{*})^{c})\leq{\Pr}^{*}\!\big((E_{n}^{*})^{c}\cap E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)}\big)+I_{(E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)})^{c}}.

Since
\Pr(E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)})\rightarrow 1
, we have
I_{(E_{n}^{(c+\gamma)})^{c}}\to_{p}0
,
and therefore
{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n}^{*})^{c})\to_{p}0
.
Consequently,

{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c})\leq{\Pr}^{*}(E_{n}^{c})+{\Pr}^{*}((E_{n}^{*})^{c})\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

From (
A.90
), combining (
A.102
), (
A.105
),
(
A.106
), and using Boole’s inequality, for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr\Big(\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|>\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\Big(\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*}+Y_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]-\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|>\varepsilon/4\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pr\Big(\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[(\varphi(\bm{D}_{n}X_{n}^{*})-\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*}))\,I_{G_{n}}\big]\Big|>\varepsilon/4\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pr\Big(\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\bm{D}_{0}X_{n}^{*})\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|>\varepsilon/4\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Pr\Big(2C_{\varphi}\,{\Pr}^{*}(G_{n}^{c})>\varepsilon/4\Big)\ \rightarrow\ 0.

Therefore, for every bounded
L
-Lipschitz
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
,

\Big|\E^{*}\!\big[\varphi(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))\big]-\E\big[\varphi(Z_{P})\big]\Big|\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0,

(A.108)

which is equivalent to

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\ \rightarrow_{d}\ N\!\big(0,\,\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\big),

in probability.
∎

Proposition 14
.

Let us assume

(C1)

The map
\pi
is differentiable in
\bm{\theta}
and
\bm{\eta}
at

(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
with full-rank Jacobian

\bm{A}_{0}=\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})}
, and let

\bm{K}_{0}:=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}
.

(C2)

The estimator
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}
satisfies

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n},

where
\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

(C3)

For
the bootstrap estimator
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}
we have

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}

\displaystyle=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}.

where
{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0
.

(C4)

Consider
Z_{\pi}\sim N(\mathbf{0},\bm{\Sigma})
such that

\sqrt{n}\bigl(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\bigr)\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\pi},

and

\sqrt{n}\bigl(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\bigr)\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\pi}\quad\text{in probability.}

Then the bootstrap distribution of

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})
is consistent for the distribution of

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})
in the Kolmogorov-Smirnov distance, i.e.,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\Big|\Pr\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\leqslant x\bigr)-{\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\leqslant x\,\bigr)\Big|\rightarrow_{p}0.

Proof.

Assumption (C2) gives the linear expansion

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n},\qquad\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Thus,

\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})=\bm{K}_{0}\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\sqrt{n}\,\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n},

and,
\sqrt{n}\,\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}0
. By (C4), and the continuous mapping theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
,

\sqrt{n}\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi}:=\bm{K}_{0}Z_{\pi}\sim N\bigl(\mathbf{0},\bm{K}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\bigr),

and by Slutsky’s theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
,

\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi}.

(A.109)

From (C3),

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*},

and subtracting the (C2) expansion for
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}
gives

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+(\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}).

Thus

\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})=\sqrt{n}\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\sqrt{n}\,(\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}).

(A.110)

By the triangle
inequality,

\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|\leq\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|+\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|.

Therefore,

\Bigl\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|>\varepsilon\Bigr\}\subseteq\Bigl\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|>\varepsilon/2\Bigr\}\ \cup\ \Bigl\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon/2\Bigr\}.

Taking conditional probabilities
{\Pr}^{*}
and using that
\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}
does not
depend on the bootstrap resample, we obtain

{\Pr}^{*}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|>\varepsilon\Bigr)\leq I_{\{\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|>\varepsilon/2\}}+{\Pr}^{*}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon/2\Bigr).

Since
\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|\rightarrow_{p}0
, the indicator term converges in
probability to
0
. Moreover, by assumption,

{\Pr}^{*}\Bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon/2\Bigr)\rightarrow_{p}0.

Thus, for every
\varepsilon>0

{\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}\|>\varepsilon\bigr)\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

(A.111)

By (C4),

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\pi}\quad\text{in probability}.

This means that for every bounded
L
-Lipschitz function

\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
we have

\big|\E^{*}[\varphi(\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big))]-\E[\varphi(Z_{\pi})]\big|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

(A.112)

Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be any bounded
L
-Lipschitz function,
L<\infty
,
and define
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
\varphi(x):=\psi(\bm{K}_{0}x)
,
for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
Since
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is bounded, say
|\psi(u)|\leqslant M
,
M<\infty
, for all

u\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
, we immediately have

|\varphi(x)|=|\psi(\bm{K}_{0}x)|\leqslant M,

so
\varphi
is bounded.
As
\psi
is
L
-Lipschitz, for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
,

|\psi(x)-\psi(y)|\leqslant L\|x-y\|.

Thus, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
,

|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|=|\psi(\bm{K}_{0}x)-\psi(\bm{K}_{0}y)|\leqslant L\,\|\bm{K}_{0}(x-y)\|\leqslant L\,\|\vect(\bm{K}_{0})\|\,\|x-y\|.

Thus
\varphi
is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant

L\,\|\vect(\bm{K}_{0})\|<\infty
.
Hence
\varphi
is a bounded
L
-Lipschitz function.
So, by using (
A.112
), we have

\big|\E^{*}[\psi(\sqrt{n}\bm{K}_{0}(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})))]-\E[\psi(\tilde{Z}_{\pi})]\big|=\big|\E^{*}[\varphi(\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big))]-\E[\varphi(Z_{\pi})]\big|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

Since this holds for every bounded Lipschitz
\psi
, we conclude that

\sqrt{n}\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

(A.113)

in probability.

Define

U_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big),\qquad V_{n}^{*}:=\sqrt{n}\,(\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}-\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}).

We now prove that
U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*}\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi}
in probability.
Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be bounded and
L
-Lipschitz, with

|\psi(x)|\leqslant M
,
M<\infty
.
Then

\displaystyle\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})]-\E[\psi(\tilde{Z}_{\pi})]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*})]\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*})]-\E[\psi(\tilde{Z}_{\pi})]\big|.

(A.114)

The second term converges to
0
in probability by the convergence of

U_{n}^{*}
to
\tilde{Z}_{\pi}
in (
A.113
). For the first term, using the Jensen inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\big|\E^{*}\!\left[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})\right]-\E^{*}\!\left[\psi(U_{n}^{*})\right]\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\E^{*}\!\left[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\right]\big|\leqslant\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\right].

Now fix
\delta>0
and split the expectation according to the law of total expectation applied to the partition

\{\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta\}
and
\{\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\}
,

\displaystyle\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\right]

\displaystyle=\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\,\big|\,\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta\right]{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta)

\displaystyle\quad+\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\,\big|\,\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right]{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta).

When
\{\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta\}
, by the Lipschitz property of
\psi
,

|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})|\leqslant L\,\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leqslant L\delta.

Hence

\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\,\big|\,\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta\right]{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta)\leqslant L\delta\,{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta)\leqslant L\delta,

since
{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|\leq\delta)\leqslant 1
.
When
\{\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\}
, by using the boundedness of
\psi
,

|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})|\leqslant|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})|+|\psi(U_{n}^{*})|\leqslant 2M.

Thus

\E^{*}\!\left[\big|\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})-\psi(U_{n}^{*})\big|\,\big|\,\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right]{\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta)\leq 2M\,{\Pr}^{*}\!\left(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right).

Combining the bounds above, we have

\big|\E^{*}\!\left[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})\right]-\E^{*}\!\left[\psi(U_{n}^{*})\right]\big|\leq L\delta+2M\,{\Pr}^{*}\!\left(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right).

By (
A.111
), for any

\varepsilon>0

\Pr\!\left(\,{\Pr}^{*}\!\bigl(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\bigr)>\varepsilon\,\right)\rightarrow 0.

The event

\Big\{\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*})]\big|>L\delta+2M\,\varepsilon\Big\},

can only occur if

\Big\{L\delta+2M\,{\Pr}^{*}\!\left(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right)>L\delta+2M\,\varepsilon\Big\},

which simplifies to the event
\{{\Pr}^{*}\!\left(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta\right)>\varepsilon\}
.
Thus,

\Pr\Big(\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*})]\big|>L\delta+2M\,\varepsilon\Big)\;\leq\;\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}(\|V_{n}^{*}\|>\delta)>\varepsilon\Big)\rightarrow 0.

Since
\delta,\varepsilon>0
are arbitrary, we obtain

\big|\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*})]-\E^{*}[\psi(U_{n}^{*})]\big|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0.

(A.115)

This implies from (
D
) that

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})=U_{n}^{*}+V_{n}^{*}\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

(A.116)

in probability.

Let us define for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}

F_{n}(x):=\Pr\bigl(\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\leqslant x\bigr),\qquad G_{n}(x):={\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\leqslant x\bigr).

Let us define

\Phi
as the cdf of
\tilde{Z}_{\pi}
that by construction is everywhere continuous on

\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
. Hence the set
\mathcal{S}
of continuity points of

\Phi
coincides with
\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
.
By using (
A.115
) to apply Lemma 10.11(i) of
(
Kosorok 2008
)
where

\mathcal{Y}_{n}
denotes the observed sample,
X_{n}=\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})
,
X=\tilde{Z}_{\pi}
and with the
closed set
A=\mathcal{S}=\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}
, we obtain

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\bigl|G_{n}(x)-\Phi(x)\bigr|\ \rightarrow_{p}\ 0.

In addition from Lemma 2.11 of
Van der Vaart 2000
, (
A.109
) implies

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\bigl|F_{n}(x)-\Phi(x)\bigr|\ \rightarrow\ 0,

Thus

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}|F_{n}(x)-G_{n}(x)|\leqslant\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\bigl|F_{n}(x)-\Phi(x)\bigr|+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\bigl|G_{n}(x)-\Phi(x)\bigr|.

and

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}|F_{n}(x)-G_{n}(x)|\rightarrow_{p}0.

∎

Theorem 1

(Restated)
.

Assume the following conditions hold.

(D1)

Let
\{F_{\bm{\theta}}:\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}\}
be a
d
-dimensional
parametric family. That is, for each

\bm{\theta}=(\bm{\mu}^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma})^{T})^{T}\in\bm{\Theta}
,
a random sample

\bm{x}_{1,\bm{\theta}},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n,\bm{\theta}}
from
F_{\bm{\theta}}

admits the representation

\bm{x}_{i,\bm{\theta}}=G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,n
,
where
\bm{u}_{1},\ldots,\bm{u}_{n}
are i.i.d. random vectors with
known common distribution
P_{U}
that does not depend on
\bm{\theta}
,
and
\E\|\bm{u}_{1}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Assume moreover, that there exists a measurable function

m:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
with
\E[m(\bm{u}_{1})^{2}]<\infty

such that for all
\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\bm{\Theta}
and all

\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
,

\|G(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{u})-G(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{u})\|\leq m(\bm{u})\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|.

The observed sample
\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{n}
consists of i.i.d. observations drawn from
F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
, where

\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\interior(\bm{\Theta})
.

(D2)

Let
\bm{H}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}
be compact, and let the tuning parameters
\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
. Moreover, the tuning parameter estimator
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
, computed from the observed sample,
satisfies

\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}-\bm{\eta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

(D3)

There exists a constant
M<\infty
such that for all
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and all

\bm{\eta}\in\bm{H}
,

\|a_{F}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})\|\leqslant M
, and

\|b_{F}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})\|\leqslant M
.

(D4)

The function
a
is Lipschitz continuous, that is, there exists an
L_{a}<\infty
such that
for any
\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
\bm{\eta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{2}\in\bm{H}
,

\|a_{F}(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1})-a_{F}(\bm{x}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2})\|\leq L_{a}\bigl(\|\bm{x}_{1}-\bm{x}_{2}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\bigr).

An analogous Lipschitz condition holds for
b_{F}(\bm{x},\bm{\eta})
.

(D5)

There exists a constant
c>0
such that for all
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
,
\min_{j}|(\mu_{b})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c
.
Define

E_{n}:=\Big\{\inf_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}}\ \min_{j}\big|(\bar{b}_{n})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\big|\geqslant c\Big\}
, and assume that there exists
\alpha>1/2
such that
\Pr(E_{n}^{c})=O(n^{-\alpha})

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and
\tilde{\delta}\leqslant c
.

(D6)

The number
H
of simulated datasets is selected such that
\sqrt{\frac{\log H}{H}}=o(n^{-1/2})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

(D7)

For any
\varepsilon>0
,

\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}:\ \|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|\geq\varepsilon}\|P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})-P_{F}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\|>0.

Moreover,
P_{F}
is differentiable in
\bm{\theta}
and
\bm{\eta}
at

(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})
with full-rank Jacobian

\bm{A}_{0}=\left.\frac{\partial\,P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})}
, with
\bm{K}_{0}:=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}
.

Then the bootstrap distribution of

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})
is consistent for the distribution of

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})
in the Kolmogorov-Smirnov distance, i.e.,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d(d+1)/2}}\Big|\Pr\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\leqslant x\bigr)-{\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\leqslant x\,\bigr)\Big|\rightarrow_{p}0,

where
{\Pr}^{*}
denotes probability computed under the bootstrap distribution, conditional on the observed data.

Proof.

Note that
\bm{\Theta}
is compact as it is shown in the proof of Proposition
2
.
Note that, we have the identity

\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)=\widehat{P}_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)
, for
\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}
,
and
\widehat{P}_{F}
evaluated using
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
on the observed sample concides with
\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
.
Moreover,
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
, for
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
.

The proof consists in verifying the assumptions of
Proposition
14
.
Assumption (C1) is satisfied.
To verify (C4), we apply Proposition
12
with
a=a_{F}
and

b=b_{F}
, and use the identities
\widehat{P}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
and

P(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
. This gives

\sqrt{n}\bigl(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\bigr)\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\pi},

(A.117)

where
Z_{\pi}\sim N(\bm{0},\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T})
.

Moreover, Proposition
13
with
a=a_{F}
and
b=b_{F}
,
together with the identity
\widehat{P}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})=\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})
, yields

\sqrt{n}\bigl(\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\bigr)\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\pi},

(A.118)

in probability.
Using Lemma
5
with
a=a_{F}
and
b=b_{F}
, and noting that

\widehat{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)=\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)
,

P(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})=\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})
, and
\bar{P}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)=\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)
, we obtain

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\big\|\widehat{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

(A.119)

and

\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\bm{\Theta}}\big\|\bar{\pi}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n},n)-\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

(A.120)

Fix
(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1}),(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
. Using the Lipschitz property in (D4) and Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\big\|\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\eta}_{1})-\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\E\!\big[a_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}},\bm{\eta}_{1})-a_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\E\big\|a_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}},\bm{\eta}_{1})-a_{F}(\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}},\bm{\eta}_{2})\big\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{a}\Big(\E\|\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}}-\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}}\|+\|\bm{\eta}_{1}-\bm{\eta}_{2}\|\Big).

Under (D1),
\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}}=G(\bm{\theta},\bm{u})
, and, for
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{U}
,

\|\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}}-\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}}\|=\|G(\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{u})-G(\bm{\theta}_{2},\bm{u})\|\leq m(\bm{u})\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|.

Taking expectations and using Cauchy–Schwarz, for
U\sim P_{U}
,

\E\|\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}}-\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}}\|\leq\E[m(U)]\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|\leq\sqrt{\E[m(U)^{2}]}\,\|\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\|.

Since
\E[m(U)^{2}]<\infty
, it follows that

\E\|\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{1}}-\bm{x}_{\bm{\theta}_{2}}\|\rightarrow 0
whenever
\bm{\theta}_{1}\rightarrow\bm{\theta}_{2}
.
Hence
\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
. The same argument yields continuity of
\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
on
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
.
Finally, by (D5) we have
\min_{j}|(\mu_{b,F})_{j}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})|>c>0
for all
(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\in\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
.
Componentwise division by a function bounded away from zero is continuous; therefore

P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
.
Since
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=P_{F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
, this proves that
\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})
is continuous on
\bm{\Theta}\times\bm{H}
.
Hence, the conditions of Proposition
9
are satisfied,
and we conclude that

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n},\qquad\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

This establishes assumption (C2).

Note that (
A.118
), combined with the same argument used in the
proof of Proposition
13
to obtain
(
A.94
), implies that for each
\varepsilon>0
there exists
an
L>0
such that

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\big(\sqrt{n}\,\|\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\widehat{\pi}_{n}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\|>L\big)>\varepsilon\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.121)

Together with (
A.119
), (
A.120
), the
conditions of Proposition
11
are
satisfied.
Hence,

\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{K}_{0}\big(\widehat{\pi}_{n}^{*}(\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})-\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n})\big)+\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*},

where
{\Pr}^{*}(\sqrt{n}\,\|\tilde{\bm{r}}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0
.
This guarantees assumption (C3).
∎

For cellMR regression we now add the condition

(D8)
\lambda_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
.

This condition is quite natural because we will apply
Corollary
1

to the II estimator, that by its definition
satisfies the constraint

c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}})\leqslant C
from which
the same property follows for its submatrix

\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{x}
, that is,

c\leqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{x})\leqslant\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{x})\leqslant C
.
So if the imposed
c
and
C
are chosen such
that
C/c
is small enough, we could even put

\lambda_{n}=0
.

Proof of Corollary
1
.

For
(\bm{\mu},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}))\in\bm{\Theta}
, define the selection matrices
\bm{S}_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d}
and

\bm{S}_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
such that

\bm{\mu}_{x}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}
,
\bm{\mu}_{y}=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}
and

\bm{\Sigma}_{x}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}
,
\bm{\Sigma}_{xy}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{y}^{T}
.
Define for
\lambda\geqslant 0
,

\bm{B}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda):=\bigl(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{y}^{T}),\qquad\bm{b}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda):=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}-\bm{B}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)^{T}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}),

and set

\psi(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda):=\bigl((\bm{b}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda))^{T},\ \vect(\bm{B}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda))^{T}\bigr)^{T}.

Let
\varphi(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda):=\bm{a}^{T}\psi(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)
, then by construction,
\widehat{\theta}_{n}=\varphi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\lambda_{n})
,
\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}=\varphi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\lambda_{n})
,
and
\theta_{0}=\varphi(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)
.

Since
(\bm{\mu},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}))\in\bm{\Theta}
, we have

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\geqslant c>0
. As
\bm{S}_{x}
selects the

x
-coordinates,
\bm{\Sigma}_{x}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}
is a principal submatrix
of
\bm{\Sigma}
. Hence, by the Cauchy interlacing theorem,

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{x})\geqslant\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})\geqslant c
.
Therefore, for every
\lambda\geqslant 0
,

\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma}_{x}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p})\geqslant c+\lambda\geqslant c
,
so that
\bm{\Sigma}_{x}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}
is symmetric positive definite and
invertible. The map

(A,C,\lambda)\mapsto(A+\lambda\bm{I}_{p})^{-1}C

is continuously differentiable on the open set where
A+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}
is
invertible, because it is the composition of matrix inversion and matrix
multiplication. Moreover,

(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma})\mapsto\bigl(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{x}^{T},\ \bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{y}^{T}\bigr)

is linear in
\bm{\Sigma}
and hence continuously differentiable. It follows by
the chain rule that

(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)\mapsto\bm{B}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)

is continuously differentiable in a neighbourhood of
(\bm{\mu}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{0},0)
.
An analogous argument applies to
\bm{b}(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)
, and therefore

\psi(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma},\lambda)
is continuously differentiable at

(\bm{\mu}_{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{0},0)
. Hence

\varphi(\bm{\theta},\lambda)=\bm{a}^{T}\psi(\bm{\theta},\lambda)
is differentiable at

(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)
.

Let

\bm{J}_{0}:=\left.\frac{\partial\,\varphi(\bm{\theta},\lambda)}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\lambda)=(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)},\qquad\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}:=\left.\frac{\partial\,\varphi(\bm{\theta},\lambda)}{\partial\lambda}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\lambda)=(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)}.

By a first-order Taylor expansion around
(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}

\displaystyle=\varphi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n},\lambda_{n})-\varphi(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})+\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}\lambda_{n}+\bm{r}_{n},

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}-\theta_{0}

\displaystyle=\varphi(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*},\lambda_{n})-\varphi(\bm{\theta}_{0},0)

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0})+\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}\lambda_{n}+\bm{r}_{n}^{*},

where
\bm{r}_{n}=o_{p}\bigl(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|+\lambda_{n}\bigr)
.
Since
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}\rightarrow_{p}\bm{\theta}_{0}
and

\lambda_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
, in particular
\lambda_{n}\rightarrow 0
, this
remainder bound follows by the same arguments used to obtain
(
A.26
) in the proof of
Proposition
9
. Moreover, since

\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
from (
A.29
) and

\lambda_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
, we have
\bm{r}_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.
By using the fact that, for any

\varepsilon>0
,

{\Pr}^{*}(\|\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\|>\varepsilon)\rightarrow_{p}0,

and the same arguments used to obtain (
A.71
) in the
proof of Proposition
11
, for every
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}\|>\varepsilon\bigr)>\delta\Big)\rightarrow 0,

as
\lambda_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
.

Subtracting the two expansions yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})+\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}\lambda_{n}-\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}\lambda_{n}+(\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{r}_{n})

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})+(\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{r}_{n}),

where the ridge terms cancel because the same tuning parameter
\lambda_{n}
is
used in the original and bootstrap estimators. Hence

\sqrt{n}\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}\bigr)=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})+\sqrt{n}(\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{r}_{n}).

Using the same arguments leading to (
A.111
) in the proof of
Proposition
14
, for every
\varepsilon,\eta>0
,

\Pr\Big({\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}\,\|\bm{r}_{n}^{*}-\bm{r}_{n}\|>\varepsilon\bigr)>\eta\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(A.122)

From the proof of Proposition
14
,

\sqrt{n}\,(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

where
\tilde{Z}_{\pi}\sim N\bigl(0,\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\bigr)
.
By the continuous mapping theorem
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
,

\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\tilde{Z}_{\pi}.

Moreover, since
\lambda_{n}=o(n^{-1/2})
,

\sqrt{n}\,\lambda_{n}\,\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}=o(1).

Therefore, by Slutsky’s lemma
(
Van der Vaart 2000
)
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})+\sqrt{n}\,\lambda_{n}\,\dot{\varphi}_{\lambda,0}+\sqrt{n}\,\bm{r}_{n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow_{d}\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\tilde{Z}_{\pi}.

Moreover, again from Proposition
14
,

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\rightarrow_{d}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

in probability.
Applying the same arguments used to obtain (
A.113
), we have

\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\sqrt{n}(\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n})\;\rightarrow_{d}\;\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

in probability. Together with (
A.122
), this implies that

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})\;\rightarrow_{d}\;\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\tilde{Z}_{\pi},

in probability, by using the same arguments to obtain
(
A.116
).

Using the same argument based on Lemma 10.11(i) of

(
Kosorok 2008
)
as in the final step of the proof of
Proposition
14
, we conclude that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\Pr\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\leqslant x\bigr)-{\Pr}^{*}\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})\leqslant x\bigr)\Big|\rightarrow_{p}0.

∎

Proof of Corollary
2
.

Let
F_{n}^{*}
denote the conditional distribution function of

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})
, i.e.,
F_{n}^{*}(x):={\Pr}^{*}\!\big(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})\leqslant x\big)
.
From the last part of the proof of Corollary
1
, we know that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|F_{n}^{*}(x)-\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)|\rightarrow_{p}0
, where
\Phi_{\bm{J}}
is the cumulative distribution of normal distribution
N(0,\sigma_{\theta}^{2})
, with
\sigma_{\theta}^{2}=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\bm{J}_{0}
. We also know that
\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\theta}
, where
Z_{\theta}\sim N(0,\sigma_{\theta}^{2})
.
Define
\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)
denote the
\gamma
-quantile of the conditional distribution of
\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})
,
\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma):=\inf\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\;F_{n}^{*}(x)\geqslant\gamma\}
,
for
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and let
q_{\gamma}:=\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma)=\inf\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\,\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)\geqslant\gamma\}
.

Fix
\gamma\in(0,1)
and let

0<\varepsilon<\min\{\gamma,1-\gamma\}
.
Suppose that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|F_{n}^{*}(x)-\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)\bigr|<\varepsilon,

that is,

\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)-\varepsilon<F_{n}^{*}(x)<\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)+\varepsilon\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}.

(A.123)

Let
x<\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon)
.
By definition of the generalized inverse,

\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)<\gamma-\varepsilon
.
Using (
A.123
),

F_{n}^{*}(x)<\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)+\varepsilon<(\gamma-\varepsilon)+\varepsilon=\gamma.

Hence no such
x
can satisfy
F_{n}^{*}(x)\geq\gamma
, and therefore

\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\geq\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon).

Let
x_{1}:=\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon)
.
Then

\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x_{1})\geq\gamma+\varepsilon.

Again using (
A.123
),

F_{n}^{*}(x_{1})>\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x_{1})-\varepsilon\geq(\gamma+\varepsilon)-\varepsilon=\gamma.

Thus
x_{1}\in\{x:\,F_{n}^{*}(x)\geq\gamma\}
, and by definition of the infimum,

\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\leq x_{1}=\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon).

Combining the two inequalities yields

\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon)\;\leq\;\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\;\leq\;\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon).

(A.124)

Since
\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}
is continuous at
\gamma\in(0,1)
, for every

\eta>0
there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, for every

u\in(0,1)
with
|u-\gamma|<\varepsilon_{0}
,

\bigl|\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(u)-q_{\gamma}\bigr|<\eta
.
Let

0<\varepsilon<\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\gamma,1-\gamma\}
, then
\gamma\pm\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and

|\gamma\pm\varepsilon-\gamma|=\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, and hence

\bigl|\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma\pm\varepsilon)-q_{\gamma}\bigr|<\eta.

This implies

q_{\gamma}-\eta<\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon)\leq\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon)<q_{\gamma}+\eta.

(A.125)

Consider the event

\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|F_{n}^{*}(x)-\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)\bigr|<\varepsilon\right\},

by applying (
A.124
),

\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon)\;\leq\;\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\;\leq\;\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon),

and from (
A.125
)

q_{\gamma}-\eta<\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma-\varepsilon)\leq\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\leq\Phi_{\bm{J}}^{-1}(\gamma+\varepsilon)<q_{\gamma}+\eta,

and hence

\bigl|\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)-q_{\gamma}\bigr|<\eta.

Therefore,

\Pr\!\left(|\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)-q_{\gamma}|>\eta\right)\leq\Pr\!\left(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|F_{n}^{*}(x)-\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)\bigr|\geq\varepsilon\right).

Since

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|F_{n}^{*}(x)-\Phi_{\bm{J}}(x)\bigr|\;\rightarrow_{p}\;0,

we conclude that

\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)\;\rightarrow_{p}\;q_{\gamma}\qquad\text{for every }\gamma\in(0,1).

Noting that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\hat{c}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)
is defined as

\hat{c}_{n}^{*}(\gamma):=\inf\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\;{\Pr}^{*}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}\leqslant x)\geqslant\gamma\Big\},

and because
\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}=\widehat{\theta}_{n}+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\,*}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})
,
we have

\hat{c}_{n}^{*}(\gamma)=\widehat{\theta}_{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\,\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\gamma).

Therefore,

\{\theta_{0}\in C_{n}\}=\Big\{\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\leqslant\sqrt{n}(\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}_{n})\leqslant\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\Big\}=\Big\{-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\leqslant\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\leqslant-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\Big\}.

Hence

\Pr\{\theta_{0}\in C_{n}\}=\Pr\!\left(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\leqslant-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\right)-\Pr\!\left(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})<-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\right).

By Slutsky’s theorem,

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})+\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\to_{d}Z_{\theta}+q_{\alpha/2}.

Since
Z_{\theta}+q_{\alpha/2}
is Gaussian, its distribution function is continuous
everywhere, in particular at
0
. Hence, by the definition of convergence in
distribution (convergence of cdfs at continuity points),

\displaystyle\Pr\!\left(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\leqslant-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\right)=

\displaystyle\Pr\!\left(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})+\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(\alpha/2)\leqslant 0\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\Pr(Z_{\theta}+q_{\alpha/2}\leqslant 0)=\Pr(Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{\alpha/2}).

Similarly, using
\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\rightarrow_{p}q_{1-\alpha/2}
,

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})+\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\to_{d}Z_{\theta}+q_{1-\alpha/2},

and

\Pr\!\left(\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\leqslant-\hat{q}_{n}^{*}(1-\alpha/2)\right)\rightarrow\Pr(Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{1-\alpha/2}).

Because
Z_{\theta}
has a continuous distribution, we also have

\Pr(Z_{\theta}<-q_{1-\alpha/2})=\Pr(Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{1-\alpha/2})
,
and therefore by combining the two limits gives

\displaystyle\Pr\{\theta_{0}\in C_{n}\}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Pr(Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{\alpha/2})-\Pr(Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{1-\alpha/2})

\displaystyle=\Pr\big(-q_{1-\alpha/2}\leqslant Z_{\theta}\leqslant-q_{\alpha/2}\big).

As
Z_{\theta}
is symmetric about
0
, then
-q_{1-u}=q_{u}
for
u\in(0,1)
,
so the last probability equals

\Pr\Big\{q_{\alpha/2}\leqslant Z_{\theta}\leqslant q_{1-\alpha/2}\Big\}=1-\alpha,

which proves (
21
).
∎

Appendix E
Proofs: Influence Functions of cellBoot

We consider the contamination model (
A.4
) and the same setting as introduced in Section
B
of the Supplementary Material, with
H_{0}:=F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
.
Given a
d
-dimensional vector
\bm{\mu}
and a
d\times d
matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
,
define the
selection matrices
\bm{S}_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d}
and

\bm{S}_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
that extract the first
p
and the last
q
components of
\bm{\mu}
such that

\bm{\mu}_{x}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}
,
\bm{\mu}_{y}=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}
and

\bm{\Sigma}_{x}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}
,
\bm{\Sigma}_{xy}=\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{S}_{y}^{T}
, represent the usual partitions of
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\Sigma}
.
Recall that
\widehat{\theta}_{n}=\bigl(\bm{\widehat{b}}^{T},\ \vect(\bm{\widehat{B}})^{T}\bigr)\bm{a}
, where

\bm{\widehat{B}}=\bigl(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{II}\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{II}\bm{S}_{y}^{T}),\qquad\bm{\widehat{b}}=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{II}-\bm{\widehat{B}}^{T}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{II}),

and
\bm{\widehat{\theta}}_{n}=\bigl((\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{II})^{T},\vecth_{s}(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{II})^{T}\bigr)^{T}
denotes the II
estimator obtained by applying to the observed sample the indirect inference procedure with
FastCellCov as auxiliary estimators.
The functional version of
\widehat{\theta}_{n}
is then defined, for a generic distribution
H
as

T(H):=\bigl(\bm{b}(H)^{T},\ \vect(\bm{B}(H))^{T}\bigr)\bm{a},

where

\bm{B}(H)=\bigl(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}_{II}(H)\bm{S}_{x}^{T}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\Sigma}_{II}(H)\bm{S}_{y}^{T}),\qquad\bm{b}(H)=\bm{S}_{y}\bm{\mu}_{II}(H)-\bm{B}(H)^{T}(\bm{S}_{x}\bm{\mu}_{II}(H)),

and
\bm{\mu}_{II}(H)
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{II}(H)
are the functionals corresponding to the estimators
\bm{\widehat{\mu}}_{II}
and
\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}_{II}
, respectively.
Thus,
T(H)=\varphi(\bm{\theta}(H))
, where

\bm{\theta}(H)=\bigl((\bm{\mu}_{II}(H))^{T},\ \vecth_{s}(\bm{\Sigma}_{II}(H))^{T}\bigr)^{T},

and
\varphi
denotes the mapping that transforms the pair

(\bm{\mu},\bm{\Sigma})
into the regression parameters

(\bm{b},\bm{B})
through the above formulas.

Let us consider
P_{F}(H,\bm{\eta}(H))
the functional associated with the auxiliary estimator
\widehat{P}_{F}
defined in Section
D
of the Supplementary Material, where
\bm{\eta}(H)
represents the functional corresponding to the tuning parameter estimator
\hat{\bm{\eta}}
.
Specifically for a vector
\bm{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
,

P_{F}(H,\bm{\eta})=\mu_{a,F}(H,\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H,\bm{\eta}),

with

\mu_{a,F}(H,\bm{\eta})=\E_{H}[a_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})],\qquad\mu_{b,F}(H,\bm{\eta})=\E_{H}[b_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})],

where
a_{F}
and
b_{F}
are defined in Section
D
of the Supplementary Material.
Further introduce

Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}):=\left(a_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})^{T},b_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})^{T}\right)^{T}
,
and

\mu_{Z_{F}}(H,\bm{\eta}):=\E_{H}[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})]=\left(\mu_{a,F}(H,\bm{\eta})^{T},\mu_{b,F}(H,\bm{\eta})^{T}\right)^{T}.

Then,
\bm{\theta}(H)
satisfies

P_{F}(H,\bm{\eta}(H))-\pi\bigl(\bm{\theta}(H),\bm{\eta}(H)\bigr)=\bm{0}.

(A.126)

Moreover,

\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}),

where
\mu_{Z_{F}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\E_{F_{\bm{\theta}}}[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})]=\left(\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{T},\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})^{T}\right)^{T}
, with

\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\E_{F_{\bm{\theta}}}\!\big[a_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})\big],\qquad\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}):=\E_{F_{\bm{\theta}}}\!\big[b_{F}(X,\bm{\eta})\big].

Under the appropriate regularity conditions, following the same arguments used in the proofs of Proposition
14
,
Theorem
1
, and Corollary
1
,
under
H_{0}
we have

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\rightarrow_{d}Z_{\theta},

where
Z_{\theta}\sim N(0,\sigma_{\theta,0}^{2})
and
the asymptotic variance is given by

\sigma_{\theta,0}^{2}=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\,\bm{K}_{0}\,\bm{D}_{0}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0}\,\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\,\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\,\bm{J}_{0}.

Here

\bm{D}_{0}:=\Big[\diag\big(\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1},\;-\diag\!\left(\mu_{a,F}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\right)\Big],

\bm{J}_{0}:=\left.\frac{\partial\,\varphi(\bm{\theta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}},\qquad\bm{K}_{0}:=\left(\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}}\right)^{-1},

and

\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0}:=\Cov_{H_{0}}\bigl(Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr),

where
\bm{\theta}_{0}:=\bm{\theta}(H_{0})
and
\bm{\eta}_{0}:=\bm{\eta}(H_{0})
.

Consider the functional

\sigma_{\theta}(H)^{2}:=\bm{J}(H)^{T}\,\bm{K}(H)\,\bm{D}(H)\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}(H)\,\bm{D}(H)^{T}\,\bm{K}(H)^{T}\,\bm{J}(H).

with
\sigma_{\theta}(H_{0})=\sigma_{\theta,0}
.

Here

\bm{J}(H):=\left.\frac{\partial\,\varphi(\bm{\theta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}(H)},\qquad\bm{K}(H)=\left(\left.\frac{\partial\,\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}(H)}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}(H)}\right)^{-1},

\bm{D}(H):=\Big[\diag\bigl(\mu_{b,F}(H,\bm{\eta}(H))\bigr)^{-1},\;-\diag\!\left(\mu_{a,F}(H,\bm{\eta}(H))\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H,\bm{\eta}(H))^{2}\right)\Big],

and

\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}(H):=\Cov_{H}\bigl(Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}(H))\bigr).

Proposition 0

(Restated)
.

Assume that the following conditions hold.

(E1)

Under
H_{0}
,

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})\rightarrow_{d}N(0,\sigma_{\theta,0}^{2}),

with
\sigma_{\theta,0}^{2}>0
.

(E2)

For
\gamma\in\{\alpha/2,\,1-\alpha/2\}
,

\frac{Q_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\gamma)}{\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}=\frac{Q_{H_{0}}(\gamma)}{\sigma_{\theta}(H_{0})}+o(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\downarrow 0,

(A.127)

and
\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})=O(1)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
.

Then

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},m,H_{0})=\bm{m}_{c}^{T}Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0}),

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},m,H_{0})=d\bm{m}_{c}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})]\right),

and

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha},H_{0})=2z_{1-\alpha/2}\Big[m_{s,1}

\displaystyle+\bm{m}_{s,2}^{T}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta},H_{0})

\displaystyle+\bm{m}_{s,3}^{T}Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})+\bm{m}_{s,4}^{T}\!\bigl(Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})\otimes Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr)\Big],

\displaystyle\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha},H_{0})=2z_{1-\alpha/2}\Big[dm_{s,1}+

\displaystyle\bm{m}_{s,2}^{T}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta},H_{0})+d\bm{m}_{s,3}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}\!\big[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})\big]\right)

\displaystyle+d\bm{m}_{s,4}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}\!\bigl[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})\otimes Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})\bigr]\right)\Big].

where
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product,

\bm{\eta}_{0}:=\bm{\eta}(H_{0})
with
\bm{\eta}(\cdot)
the functional corresponding to the tuning parameters estimator
\bm{\widehat{\eta}}_{n}
in the auxiliary estimator,
H(j,\bm{c})
is the distribution of

X\sim H_{0}
but with its
j
-th component fixed
at the constant
c_{j}
, and
z_{\gamma}
denotes the
\gamma
-quantile of the standard normal
distribution.
The quantities
\bm{m}_{c}
,
m_{s,1}
,
\bm{m}_{s,2}
,
\bm{m}_{s,3}
and
\bm{m}_{s,4}
are
defined in the proof, and

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta},H_{0})
and
\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta},H_{0})

are the casewise and cellwise influence functions of

\bm{\eta}
.

Proof of Proposition
4
.

Assumption (E1) implies

\sqrt{n}\{T(\hat{H}_{0})-T(H_{0})\}\rightarrow_{d}N(0,\sigma_{\theta,0}^{2})
, where
\hat{H}_{0}
is the empirical
distribution obtained from an i.i.d. sample of size
n
drawn from
H_{0}
.
This implies

\frac{Q_{H_{0}}(\gamma)}{\sigma_{\theta}(H_{0})}=z_{\gamma},\qquad\gamma\in\{\alpha/2,1-\alpha/2\}.

Combining this with (
A.127
) yields for
\gamma\in\{\alpha/2,1-\alpha/2\}
,

\frac{Q_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\gamma)}{\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}=z_{\gamma}+o(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\downarrow 0.

As
\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})=O(1)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, it follows that

Q_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\gamma)=\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})\,z_{\gamma}+o(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\downarrow 0.

Subtracting the expansions for
\gamma\in\{\alpha/2,1-\alpha/2\}
yields

\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha}(H_{\varepsilon})=\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})\{z_{1-\alpha/2}-z_{\alpha/2}\}+o(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\downarrow 0.

Since
z_{1-\alpha/2}=-z_{\alpha/2}
, the bracket simplifies to
2z_{1-\alpha/2}
, and hence

\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha}(H_{\varepsilon})=2z_{1-\alpha/2}\,\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})+o(\varepsilon),\qquad\varepsilon\downarrow 0.

When the distribution
H_{\varepsilon}
is of the form
H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})
,
differentiating with respect to
\varepsilon
at
\varepsilon=0
yields

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha},H_{0})=2z_{1-\alpha/2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},\sigma_{\theta},H_{0}).

(A.128)

Similarly, differentiating the definition of
m
gives

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},m,H_{0})=\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},T,H_{0}).

(A.129)

In the cellwise contamination setting, where
H_{\varepsilon}
is denoted by

H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c})
, an analogous result follows, that is

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\widetilde{\ell}_{\alpha},H_{0})=2z_{1-\alpha/2}\,\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},\sigma_{\theta},H_{0}).

(A.130)

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},m,H_{0})=\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},T,H_{0}).

(A.131)

Let consider the functional
T
and the contaminated distribution
H_{\varepsilon}
.
Then

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}T\bigl(H_{\varepsilon}\bigr)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Differentiating (
A.126
), we have

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\left(P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))-\pi\bigl(\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon}),\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})\bigr)\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{0},

and thus

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{K}_{0}\Bigg[\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\left.\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{\bm{\eta}=\bm{\eta}_{0}}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\Bigg].

Note that

\left.\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}}=\bm{D}_{0}\,\left.\frac{\partial\mu_{Z_{F}}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta}_{0})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}}.

Note that
H_{0}=F_{\bm{\theta}_{0}}
, thus

\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})=P_{F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}).

Then

\left.\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{\bm{\eta}=\bm{\eta}_{0}}=\left.\frac{\partial P_{F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{\bm{\eta}=\bm{\eta}_{0}}.

Moreover, by the chain rule,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\left.\frac{\partial P_{F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\eta}}\right|_{\bm{\eta}=\bm{\eta}_{0}}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Substituting into the differentiated population condition, the
\bm{\eta}
-terms cancel, and hence

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{K}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

(A.132)

Therefore,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}T(H_{\varepsilon})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\bm{K}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

(A.133)

When
H_{\varepsilon}
is either the dependent or independent
contamination models, we have, for any vector-valued function
g
,

\E_{H_{\varepsilon}}[g(X)]=\sum_{j=0}^{d}\delta_{j}(\varepsilon)\sum_{I\in\ell_{j}}\,\E_{H(I,\bm{c})}[g(X)],

(A.134)

where

\ell_{j}=\{I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\}:1\leqslant i_{1}<\cdots<i_{j}\leqslant d,\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant d\},

and where
H(I,\bm{c})
is the distribution of a random vector
X=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})
such that

X_{i}=c_{i}\ \text{if }i\in I,\qquad X_{i}=Z_{i}\ \text{if }i\notin I,

with
Z\sim H_{0}
.
In particular,
\ell_{0}=\{\emptyset\}
and
H(\emptyset,\bm{c})=H_{0}
, while

\ell_{d}=\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
and
H(\{1,2,\ldots,d\},\bm{c})=\Delta_{\bm{c}}
, the point-mass
distribution at
\bm{c}
.

Under the FDCM we have

\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)=(1-\varepsilon),\;\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)=\cdots=\delta_{d-1}(\varepsilon)=0
, and

\delta_{d}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon
.

Thus,

\E_{H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c})}[g(X)]=(1-\varepsilon)\E_{H_{0}}[g(X)]+\varepsilon\,g(\bm{c}).

Applying this to
g(X)=a_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})
and
g(X)=b_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})
yields

\mu_{Z_{F}}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})=(1-\varepsilon)\mu_{Z_{F}}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})+\varepsilon\,Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Therefore,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{Z_{F}}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\mu_{Z_{F}}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

(A.135)

Using the componentwise quotient rule for

P_{F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})=\mu_{a,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=

\displaystyle\;\diag\!\big(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{a,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle-\diag\!\Big(\mu_{a,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\Big)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{b,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\diag\!\big(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1}\big(a_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\mu_{a,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)

\displaystyle-\diag\!\Big(\mu_{a,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\Big)\big(b_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})-\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\diag\!\big(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1}a_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0})

\displaystyle-\diag\!\Big(\mu_{a,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\Big)\,b_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

Thus, we can write

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{D},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{D}_{0}Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0}).

(A.136)

Then from (
A.133
), we have

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{case}}}(\bm{c},T,H_{0})=\bm{m}_{c}^{T}Z_{F}(\bm{c},\bm{\eta}_{0}),

with
\bm{m}_{c}^{T}=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}
.

Under the FICM we have

\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)=(1-\varepsilon)^{d},\qquad\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)=d(1-\varepsilon)^{d-1}\varepsilon,

so that
\delta_{0}(0)=1
,
\delta_{1}(0)=0
,
\delta_{1}^{\prime}(0)=d
, and

\delta_{j}(0)=\delta_{j}^{\prime}(0)=0
for all
j\geq 2
.
By separating the first two terms in (
A.134
), we have

\displaystyle\E_{H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c})}[g(X)]=(1-\varepsilon)^{d}\,\E_{H_{0}}[g(X)]+d(1-\varepsilon)^{d-1}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}[g(X)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=2}^{d}\delta_{k}(\varepsilon)\sum_{I\in\ell_{k}}\E_{H(I,\bm{c})}[g(X)],

where
H(j,\bm{c}):=H(\{j\},\bm{c})
. This yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\E_{H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c})}[g(X)]\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-d\E_{H_{0}}[g(X)]+d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}[g(X)].

Applying this identity to
g(X)=a_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})
and
g(X)=b_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})
yields

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{Z_{F}}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-d\,\mu_{Z_{F}}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})+d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}\!\big[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})\big].

Using the componentwise quotient rule for

P=\mu_{a,F}\oslash\mu_{b,F}
evaluated at
\varepsilon=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=

\displaystyle\;\diag\!\big(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\big)^{-1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{a,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle-\diag\!\left(\mu_{a,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})^{2}\right)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{b,F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

And thus, by using
\bm{D}_{0}
, this can be written compactly as

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H(G_{\varepsilon}^{I},\bm{c}),\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{D}_{0}d\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})].

Then from (
A.133
), we have

\IFu_{\mbox{\scriptsize{cell}}}(\bm{c},T,H_{0})=d\bm{m}_{c}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\E_{H(j,\bm{c})}[Z_{F}(X,\bm{\eta}_{0})]\right).

Let us consider the functional
\sigma_{\theta}(\cdot)^{2}
evaluated in
H_{\varepsilon}

\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})^{2}=\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}^{T}\,\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}\,\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,\varepsilon}\,\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}^{T}\,\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}^{T}\,\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}.

where

\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}:=\bm{J}(H_{\varepsilon})
,
\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}:=\bm{K}(H_{\varepsilon})
,
\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}:=\bm{D}(H_{\varepsilon})
, and
\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,\varepsilon}:=\bm{\Sigma}_{ab}(H_{\varepsilon})
.
Let

\bm{M}_{\varepsilon}:=\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,\varepsilon}\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}^{T}\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}^{T},\qquad\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})^{2}=\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}^{T}\bm{M}_{\varepsilon}\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}.

Then

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\sigma_{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})^{2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{M}_{\varepsilon}}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{J}_{0}+2\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\bm{M}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=(\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\otimes\bm{J}_{0}^{T})\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{M}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+2\bm{J}_{0}^{T}\bm{M}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

The chain rule gives

\left.\frac{\partial\bm{J}_{\varepsilon}}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{J}_{2,0}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where

\bm{J}_{2,0}:=\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi(\bm{\theta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}\,\partial\bm{\theta}^{T}}\right|_{\bm{\theta}=\bm{\theta}_{0}},

and, from (
A.132
),

\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{K}_{0}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Differentiating
\vect(\bm{M}_{\varepsilon})

yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{M}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=

\displaystyle\;(\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0}\bm{D}_{0}^{T}\otimes\bm{I}_{d+d(d+1)/2})\,\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle+(\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0}\otimes\bm{K}_{0})\,\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{D}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle+(\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\otimes\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0})\,\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle+(\bm{K}_{0}\otimes\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0})\,\bm{P}_{D}\,\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{D}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle+(\bm{I}_{d+d(d+1)/2}\otimes\bm{K}_{0}\bm{D}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{ab,0}\bm{D}_{0}^{T})\,\bm{P}_{K}\,\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0},

where
\bm{P}_{D}
and
\bm{P}_{K}
are the permutation matrices satisfying
\vect(\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}^{T})=\bm{P}_{D}\vect(\bm{D}_{\varepsilon})
and
\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}^{T})=\bm{P}_{K}\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon})
.
Let

\bm{K}_{\varepsilon}=\left(\left.\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon}),\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))}\right)^{-1}=:\bm{G}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}.

Using the identity
\partial\bm{G}^{-1}=-\bm{G}^{-1}(\partial\bm{G})\bm{G}^{-1}
, we obtain

\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-(\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\otimes\bm{K}_{0})\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{G}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

For each
j=1,\dots,d+d(d+1)/2
, define

\bm{A}_{j}:=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\theta}^{T}}\Big(\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\Big)\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})},\qquad(\bm{A}_{j})_{ik}=\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{i}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\theta_{j}\,\partial\theta_{k}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})},

\bm{B}_{j}:=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\eta}^{T}}\Big(\frac{\partial\pi(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\Big)\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+d(d+1)/2)\times r},\qquad(\bm{B}_{j})_{i\ell}=\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\pi_{i}(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})}{\partial\theta_{j}\,\partial\eta_{\ell}}\right|_{(\bm{\theta},\bm{\eta})=(\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0})}.

Introduce the stacked matrices

\bm{H}_{\theta\theta}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{A}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{A}_{d+d(d+1)/2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{H}_{\theta\eta}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{B}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\bm{B}_{d+d(d+1)/2}\end{pmatrix}.

Then, stacking the derivatives by rows and using vectorization,

\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{G}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\bm{H}_{\theta\theta}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\bm{H}_{\theta\eta}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Thus,

\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{K}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-(\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\otimes\bm{K}_{0})\bm{H}_{\theta\theta}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\theta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-(\bm{K}_{0}^{T}\otimes\bm{K}_{0})\bm{H}_{\theta\eta}\left.\frac{\partial\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

Write

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\Big[\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon})))^{-1},\;-\diag\!\big(\mu_{a,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\oslash\mu_{b,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))^{2}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=:[\bm{D}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon},\bm{D}^{(2)}_{\varepsilon}],

and hence

\vect(\bm{D}_{\varepsilon})=\begin{pmatrix}\vect(\bm{D}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon})\\
\vect(\bm{D}^{(2)}_{\varepsilon})\end{pmatrix}.

Using
d(A^{-1})=-A^{-1}(dA)A^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\bm{D}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=

\displaystyle\;-\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\diag\!\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{b,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}.

Vectorizing yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\vect(\bm{D}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=-\big(\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}\otimes\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\bm{L}_{d+d(d+1)/2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{b,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=-\bm{B}^{D}_{1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mu_{b,F}(H_{\varepsilon},\bm{\eta}(H_{\varepsilon}))\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

with
\bm{B}^{D}_{1}:=\big(\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}\otimes\diag(\mu_{b,F}(H_{0},\bm{\eta}_{0}))^{-1}\big)\bm{L}_{d+d(d+1)/2}
and
